Vertin: Sonetto, you're the only one who knows the details of the mission besides myself. One of us should stay in a safe place.
维尔汀：十四行诗，你知道我来别列格勒的任务细节。我们至少要有一个人留在安全的地方。

Lilya: Relax. I'll bring Vertin back in one piece.
红弩箭：别担心，有我在，保管会把维尔汀完完整整地带回来。

Sonetto: Understood. Please be careful, Timekeeper. I'll prepare a teleport disk for you.
十四行诗：……我明白了。司辰，请千万要小心。在你走之前，我会为你准备一张传送软盘。

Commander Ivan: So it stands. I'll send a rider to Igor's messenger.
军长伊万：那眼下的方案就是这样了。我去派人回复伊戈尔的信使。

Commander Ivan: We meet on the open plain, within striking distance of both sides.
军长伊万：会面地点就在两军中间的开阔地上，双方都在各自的火力打击范围之内。

In that space between armies, the air seems to tighten under the antagonistic stares of the opposing sides.
在两军对峙的前线，空气都仿佛被互相敌视的军队所压缩。

Tens of thousands of eyes are fixed on the few figures at the center of the valley. They walk toward each other across the open grassland.
数万双眼睛紧盯着谷地中孤零零的数人——在空旷的草地中，他们以步行互相接近。

Vertin: ...
维尔汀：……

Igor arrives first at the designated location and waits patiently.
伊戈尔先一步抵达预定的位置，他颇有耐心地在原地等待。

He stands alone, not a single guard at his side.
他孤身一人，没有任何护卫跟随。

Igor: I'm sorry that this is the way we meet again.
伊戈尔：不得不以这种方式与你们再次见面，我感到很遗憾。

Vertin: I feel the same, Igor. What did you want to talk about?
维尔汀：我也感到遗憾，伊戈尔——你想和我谈什么？

The rebel leader seems unfazed by his opponent's omission of rank.
叛军首领对于自己被忽略的军衔并不以为意。

Igor: After the last "Storm," you helped Moldir escape Antarctica and avoid investigation by the Foundation. You have my thanks.
伊戈尔：“暴雨”后，你帮助莫莉德尔避开了基金会的搜查，安全离开南极。我需要为此向你表达我的谢意。

Vertin: She provided us valuable support. I simply returned the favor.
维尔汀：她为我们的计划带来了额外助力，这是对她的报偿。

Lilya: If that's all you got to say, then send a thank-you letter. Get to the point: what do you want?
红弩箭：如果只是为了表达感谢，这个阵仗也太大费周章了。让我问得更直接点：你的目的是什么？

Igor glances at Ivan, who watches in silence.
伊戈尔看向伊万，对方一直谨慎地缄口不语，只是在观察。

Igor: There are some matters I want to clarify.
伊戈尔：我只是想澄清一些事实。

Lilya: Where you want to start? From the first time you put on the Zeno uniform? Or from São Paulo?
红弩箭：事实？——你想从哪里说起，从你第一次穿上芝诺的制服开始，还是从圣保罗开始？

Igor: São Paulo. Let's start there.
伊戈尔：那就从圣保罗开始。

To everyone's surprise, the admiral lets out the city's name. The sound of it almost stings.
将军在所有人的意料之外，吐出了这个带有刺痛感的城市名字。

The corner of Ivan's eye twitches.
伊万的眼角显而易见地抽搐了一下。

Igor: Before I arrived in São Paulo, I struck a deal with the "dead" Arcana.
伊戈尔：在抵达圣保罗之前，我与“死去”的阿尔卡纳达成了一项交易。

Vertin: You communicated with Arcana after her death?
维尔汀：你是说，在阿尔卡纳第一次死亡之后，你与祂有过交流？

Igor: Yes. You witnessed the events in Antarctica. I trust there is no need to explain why I heard Her voice.
伊戈尔：是的。既然你见证了南极发生的事，那我就不必解释为什么阿尔卡纳的声音可以进入我的头脑。

Igor: Death and resurrection are part of the myth She aimed to construct. I refused to become Her follower, but I desperately needed the reward only She could provide.
伊戈尔：死亡与复活都是祂渴望构筑的神话的一环，我拒绝成为祂的使徒，但我急迫地需要祂提供的回报。

Vertin: Arcana needed Urd to complete her resurrection ritual. What did you need?
维尔汀：阿尔卡纳需要兀尔德完成复活的仪式，那你需要的是什么？

Igor: A true shelter.
伊戈尔：一份真正的庇护。

Igor: A "boon," a "hidden place," a "promise" from the Surpassing Excellence. She used many words for this little cube.
伊戈尔：一份“奇迹”，一处“秘境”，一道来自超然存在的“许诺”。祂用了很多词来修饰这一只小立方体。

Vertin: ...
维尔汀：……

Igor: Is it familiar to you, Timekeeper?
伊戈尔：它是否让你有一种熟悉感，司辰？

Vertin: The material's similar to the Tear of Comala.
维尔汀：它的材质有点类似于“科马拉之雨”……

Igor: The origin is the same, but their function is different.
伊戈尔：同源之物，但作用不尽相同。

Igor clenches his fist, and the black cube disappears.
伊戈尔手握成拳，那被用多种词语修饰的黑色立方体凭空消失。

Vertin: Why make a deal with Arcana if just to avoid the "Storm"? The Foundation already provides you shelter.
维尔汀：如果你只是为了躲避“暴雨”，根本不需要和阿尔卡纳做交易。基金会就可以为你提供庇护。

Igor: When you learn of Urd and the Amber Room, you will understand why I cannot trust the Foundation's promises. They have chosen a perilous path.
伊戈尔：而等你了解兀尔德与琥珀屋之后，就能理解我为什么不能信任基金会的诺言。他们选择了一条更险恶的路。

The name, mentioned too many times of late, plucks at a golden thread in her mind.
近期被反复提及的字眼拨动了脑中一根金色的弦。

Vertin: You know about the Amber Room too.
维尔汀：你也知道琥珀屋……

Igor: I know what led you here—Urd, Amber Room—the words appear together. But whoever gave you this intelligence likely knows little more than that.
伊戈尔：我知道你是带着怎样的线索来到别列格勒的。兀尔德，琥珀屋，两个词语拼在一起，但我相信，给你情报的人也并不知道更多。

Igor: Bereggrad is not a large city. But without guidance, you will never find the Amber Room, even if you turn it inside out.
伊戈尔：别列格勒不是个大城市，但缺少指引，就算把它翻个底朝天，你也很难找到琥珀屋所在。

Igor: Fortunately, its location is not a secret to me.
伊戈尔：但琥珀屋的地点于我而言不是一个秘密。

Igor: Because I was involved in it all.
伊戈尔：我曾是这一切的参与者。

Vertin: How much more do you know?
维尔汀：你还知道多少？

Igor: No more questions. I am no longer bound by the Foundation's confidentiality rules. I can lead you directly to the answers.
伊戈尔：不需要向我提问。我现在的身份，不受基金会保密条例的约束。我可以为你带路，让你找到答案。

Igor gives them space to consider the deal, then turns to the cavalry commander, who has not spoken a word.
伊戈尔留下一段用于思考交易价值的空白，趁此时间，他转向一直没有说话的骑兵军军长。

Igor: Ivan, I know you care deeply for the Don. I admit some responsibility for the looting of the upstream villages.
伊戈尔：我知道你更关心顿河的现状，伊万。上游的一些村庄遭到了洗劫，我的确要为此承担责任。

Commander Ivan: Then it was you?
军长伊万：那真是你的手笔？

Igor: It was Krutov.
伊戈尔：是格鲁托夫。

Commander Ivan: Krutov? Hah. He or you, what difference is there? He betrayed Zeno at your side in São Paulo. He follows your orders.
军长伊万：他？——是他做的，那又有什么不同？我知道他在圣保罗之后和你一起背叛了芝诺。

Igor: Not since Antarctica. With the new era, Krutov has taken his own path. We have some differences of opinion.
伊戈尔：在南极之后就不是了。新时代来临后，格鲁托夫不再听从我的指挥，我们之间出现了一些理念不合。

Igor: The Manus deal was a one-off, but Krutov wants more.
伊戈尔：我与重塑之手达成的，只是一项一次性的交易。但格鲁托夫的渴望已经超过了限度。

Igor: He believed our reward should be greater, that receiving only a small black cube was like pleasing a child with a toy.
伊戈尔：他觉得自己还能向重塑之手索要更多，而不是一个对他来说没有用处的黑色小盒。

Lilya: A traitor among traitors. Guess people do get what they deserve.
红弩箭：我明白了，叛军中的叛军——你的军队正在走向自我毁灭。

Igor: Sharp. You're right. I should've known that without Zeno, his desire would grow beyond my control. I will take the blame for his madness.
伊戈尔：讽刺而尖锐的评论，但我确实要为格鲁托夫的疯狂负责，我应该知道他的野心在脱离芝诺之后是不可控的。

Lilya: And for the Zeno soldiers you killed in São Paulo.
红弩箭：就像该为圣保罗那些本不应该殉职的芝诺士兵负责一样。

Igor: I will not deny it. And I will atone.
伊戈尔：我不会否认这一点，也必将为我所犯下的罪孽赎罪。

Igor: But I must first stop what Krutov has done to the Don basin.
伊戈尔：但眼下，我也必须对格鲁托夫在顿河的所作所为负责。

Igor: I trusted him—trained him under my command—but failed to see his inflating ambition. I did not snuff it when I had the chance.
伊戈尔：我曾将他视作亲信，他在我手下成长、壮大，我却没能及时发觉、遏制他膨胀的野心。

Igor: In the end, he built an army, from my soldiers and weapons, and from the Manus and other sources.
伊戈尔：他带走了一部分人员和装备，并且得到了重塑之手和其他军队的增员。

Igor: In this era, our relationship has become rancorous. He hopes to snatch the "boon," and I hope to end this matter once and for all.
伊戈尔：进入这个时代后，他和我一直在互相追逐，他希望从我手上夺走“奇迹”，而我希望永绝后患。

Commander Ivan: Then he and his bandit forces now belong to Manus Vindictae.
军长伊万：那么，他和他手上的匪军，就已经成为重塑之手的军队了。

Igor: He works with them as an ally, not a follower. He has not undergone the mask trial. Likely his arrogance is the reason.
伊戈尔：严格来说不完全是，他没有经历面具的试炼，只是作为盟友与重塑之手合作。我想这是他的傲慢使然。

Commander Ivan: Whether he has joined the Manus or not, it matters little.
军长伊万：呵，无论他是否加入了重塑之手，对我们来说都没有什么区别。

Commander Ivan: These differences of opinion among you rebels are the cause of the Don's suffering. If an ounce of duty is left in you, end it. Quickly.
军长伊万：是叛军内部利益分配不均而导致了内乱以及顿河的悲剧。如果你还有一丝责任感，就应该快点解决问题。

Igor: I will launch an attack on Krutov when the time is right, but the battle will be hard-fought.
伊戈尔：我会在时机成熟时对格鲁托夫发动进攻，但我无法轻易取胜。

The admiral frankly admits the trouble his army is facing.
将军坦率地承认军队正面临的困境。

Igor: I'm sure you already have some information, but Krutov is more powerful than you think.
伊戈尔：我相信你已经得到了他的部分情报，但或许，格鲁托夫目前的力量要比你推测的更强大。

Igor: Zeno weapons, Manus troops, the backing of Don warlords—he has them all. My army alone cannot defeat him.
伊戈尔：他手中拥有大量芝诺的武器，而且得到了重塑之手与顿河小军阀的增员，光凭我的军队很难彻底击溃他。

Igor: That is why I ask your cooperation. With the Cavalry Corps, we will outnumber them. We will have the advantage.
伊戈尔：所以，我希望与骑兵军合作，相互策应，对格鲁托夫的军队形成人数和战术上的优势。

Commander Ivan: ...
军长伊万：……

Igor's words are like a shashka sunk into the mud, waiting for the other to pull it free.
伊戈尔的话像一把插进泥土中的马刀，等待另一人拔出。

Across the plain, tens of thousands of soldiers still face one another in tense silence.
在开阔地的两端，数万人的军队仍维持着紧张的对峙。

Commander Ivan: Are you sure you know what you're doing?
军长伊万：你知道你在说什么吗，伊戈尔？

Igor: I'm sure. And I hope I have proven my sincerity.
伊戈尔：我很清楚我在做什么，而且，我想我已经展示了最大限度的诚意。

Igor: This serves all parties. You will bring peace to the Don, and the Timekeeper will learn what she seeks of Urd.
伊戈尔：合作的结果对我们都有利，顿河能重获和平，而我也能引导司辰找到与兀尔德有关的线索。

Igor: If you agree, everything will be settled within a week. You have my word.
伊戈尔：如果我们能达成共识，这一切问题都会在一周之内解决。

Igor: The sunlight is fading, so is our time. I hope the deal can be made now.
伊戈尔：时间不多，太阳快落山了。我希望能在此刻达成协定。

17  关键词 / Keyword

A Note From Sonetto
十四行诗的留言

Timekeeper,
    Regarding the slips of paper covered in unknown symbols that were tucked into Mr. Creius's letter—I spent some time analyzing them and eventually found relevant information in an old tome in the library.
    They follow an ancient and rarely used encryption method. I deciphered them and extracted several key fragments from the mass of meaningless character strings. I've transcribed them below:
 █ █∮Ω≈ Bereggrad Ψ∫λ∂∇█ █
 █ █∮Ω≈ List Ψ∫λ Amber Room ∂∇█ █
 █ █∮Ω≈ The Foundation Ψ∫λ Related ∂∇█ █
 █ █∮Ω≈ Secret Ψ∫λ Experiment ∂∇█ █
 █ █∮Ω≈ Urd Ψ∫λ∂∇█ █
司辰，
关于刻雷乌斯先生信件中夹着的写有大量未知符号的小纸条，我花费了点时间对其进行研究，并最终在图书馆的一本典籍中发现了它的相关信息。
这是一种年代久远、使用情景极少的加密规则。我对其进行逐行解密，从大量无效字符串中获得少量关键信息，已手抄至下方：
 █ █∮Ω≈别列格勒Ψ∫λ∂∇█ █
 █ █∮Ω≈名单Ψ∫λ琥珀屋∂∇█ █
 █ █∮Ω≈基金会Ψ∫λ相关∂∇█ █
 █ █∮Ω≈秘密Ψ∫λ实验∂∇█ █
 █ █∮Ω≈兀尔德Ψ∫λ∂∇█ █

18  白夜 / White Night

White Night
白夜

Under the cover of night, the Zeno Cavalry Corps resumes its march toward Bereggrad.
芝诺骑兵军再度启程，在夜色的掩护下向别列格勒而去。

The Gryphon Legion holds position by the main road to the city. Both armies remain on high alert.
狮鹫军驻守在城外的主要道路旁，两支军队在深邃的黑夜中保持着相互的警惕。

Alone on the fields comes the slow crunch of two pairs of heavy boots.
两双沉重的马靴缓慢地在草地上踏着步。

Igor: It must have taken some convincing, especially with Sternova. She's rarely easy to sway.
伊戈尔：我猜说服你手下的人一定花了你不少时间。尤其是斯捷尔诺娃，她不是一个好对付的人。

Commander Ivan: I swore it would bring peace to the Don. Sabina objected, of course. "Once a traitor, always a traitor." Her exact words.
军长伊万：我给他们的承诺是，顿河会迎来和平。但莎宾娜不这么认为，她说，“叛徒永远是叛徒，不可信任。”

Igor: She's right.
伊戈尔：她说得也没错。

Igor pulls back his sleeve. A circle of neatly inscribed symbols glows faintly on his scarred arm.
伊戈尔抬起手，在满是疤痕的小臂上，一圈刻工规整的符号在夜色中散发着微光。

Igor: The arcane incantation customized by Zeno for every arcanist commander, preventing the misuse of arcane skills.
伊戈尔：芝诺过去为每一个神秘学家将领单独定制的抑制神秘术咒文，为了防止神秘术的不合规使用。

Igor: Never thought I'd see it again.
伊戈尔：……没想到还是用上了它。

Commander Ivan: Your arcane skill can vanish an army in an instant. I must take precautions.
军长伊万：我知道你的神秘术能让一支军队从战场上消失，我不能没有防范。

Igor: Ever cautious. Good. Though, I'm surprised you still remember how to write it.
伊戈尔：谨慎是好习惯。让我意外的是，你还记得针对我的抑制咒文写法。

Commander Ivan: I knew a day would come when I'd need it. I never thought it would be for this.
军长伊万：因为我相信会有用上它的这一天，当然，这次和我想象中的情况很不一样。

The commander has no desire to pursue the topic. He looks away.
军长不想继续这个话题，他将目光从伊戈尔手臂上的符文移走。

Commander Ivan: Keep your promise. The Hawks are circling. They will devour me and Somme if given the chance.
军长伊万：“鹰派”的老东西会要了我的命，或许索姆也会受牵连。你最好真的能像你保证的那样把问题解决。

Igor: I know the Foundation wants no conflict between the cavalry and me, and the Timekeeper must have promised to vouch for you. Her words will not go unheard.
伊戈尔：我知道基金会不希望骑兵军与我发生冲突，而且司辰一定给了为你担保的承诺。她的话不会被忽视的。

Igor: Were it not for the Timekeeper's mission in Bereggrad, they would have ordered you to leave the Don immediately. No two-week buffer.
伊戈尔：如果不是司辰在别列格勒有特殊的任务，他们会命令你立刻撤离顿河，连两周的缓冲期也没有。

Commander Ivan: How did you learn this?
军长伊万：你从哪里截取的情报？

Igor: I do not need interception to know what the Joint Committee thinks, provided their membership remains unchanged.
伊戈尔：我不需要截取情报，只要在联合委员会里坐着的还是那一批人，我就能猜出他们的想法。

Igor: You're a Cossack, so they sent you here to recruit new riders. For the same reason, they do not want you stationed here in your hometown.
伊戈尔：你是哥萨克，所以他们把你送到顿河，补充兵员。但是他们不愿意让你驻留家乡，也因为你是哥萨克。

Commander Ivan: ...
军长伊万：……

Commander Ivan: Don't speak like you know me, Igor. You and I are no longer brothers.
军长伊万：不要这样和我说话，伊戈尔。我们已经不是同一条船上的人。

Commander Ivan: Demotion, imprisonment—no matter what they hang upon me, I will do what I must to defend my home.
军长伊万：不管是被降职，还是被关进监牢，我都会认命。我只是希望保证家乡的安全。

Igor's eyes follow the river upstream to Bereggrad's low rooftops gleaming beneath the starry sky.
伊戈尔的目光顺着河流，看向上游。别列格勒低沉的屋顶，在星夜的照耀下竟有如萤光闪烁。

Igor: We were born and raised on this land. We both bade farewell to our families that day, leaving Bereggrad and the steppe for the cold walls of Zeno.
伊戈尔：这里是我们长大的地方，我和你都是在别列格勒与家人告别，然后跟着同级的新人加入芝诺。

Igor: On a fine day, you can make out the church spire from here.
伊戈尔：如果是在白天，比较晴朗的时候，站在这里差不多就能看见别列格勒教堂的尖顶。

Igor: The city is not as large as it was in 99. Back then, we could easily see the suburbs too.
伊戈尔：现在城市小了很多，如果是在99年，我们站着的地方，就可以看见别列格勒的城郊。

Commander Ivan: Many things are different. All this will be washed away in time. Do not put too much weight on it.
军长伊万：河流的拐点也不一样，很多东西都有所不同。这里终究是要被洪流冲走的，别寄予太多怀念。

Igor: But the steppe remains unchanged, and so do her people. The smell of grass and dirt is no different than at the century's end.
伊戈尔：但这里的人还是一样的，草原的模样也没有改变，风里的青草和泥土味与世纪末的没有什么不同。

Commander Ivan: I still cannot trust you. Krutov is but one stain on the Don. There are many more to be blotted.
军长伊万：我还是很难相信你说的话。格鲁托夫甚至不是顿河唯一的问题，解决他也不能挽救顿河。

Commander Ivan: The Golden Famine will strip the steppe bare. Her people will either starve or leave.
军长伊万：“金色饥荒”最终会将这个时代的顿河变成一片荒芜之地，人们要么饿死，要么远走他乡。

Commander Ivan: Most will have to live off their food stores. Some poor folk have none at all.
军长伊万：这一个冬天，顿河人都只能靠上一年的存粮过日子，而一些家境稍差的人，根本没有剩余的粮食。

Igor: What does the Foundation believe to be the cause?
伊戈尔：基金会认为“金色饥荒”是由什么导致的？

Commander Ivan: They haven't investigated. The Foundation is stretched thin. The steppe is not worth their focus.
军长伊万：基金会根本匀不出人手来调查这里的异常，顿河不是战争的焦点，不值得他们关心。

Commander Ivan: Hah. Our best hope may be the next "Storm." If it is kind, the Don will wake as she should in the next era.
军长伊万：可笑的是，现在反而只能寄希望于一场“暴雨”，运气好的话，下一个时代一切又会恢复正常。

Igor lets Ivan's almost cruel joke pass.
伊戈尔没有接过伊万这显得有些残忍的玩笑。

Igor: This heat is the result of an arcane ritual.
伊戈尔：季节倒错是一场仪式。

Commander Ivan: A ritual? Bending the weather over the entire lower Don? How can it be done?
军长伊万：一场仪式？覆盖整个顿河下游地区的气候，这怎么可能？

Messenger: Commander!
传令兵：军长——！

The messenger's shout cuts the conversation short.
问题尚未得到解答，传令兵那急躁的呼喊声就插入了二位将领的谈话——这是作为传令兵的特权之一。

He runs up to them and bends over, hands on his thighs as he attempts to catch his breath.
他气喘吁吁地靠近，站稳后弯下了腰，双手撑着大腿大口喘着气。

Commander Ivan: Yes?
军长伊万：有什么要事？

The messenger's breathing is unsettled, not from weariness, but from sheer fear.
传令兵的呼吸频率很不正常，似乎不是出于劳累，而是出于面临极端险境的恐慌。

He turns to the Zeno rebel leader with a curious expression on his face.
他带着有些好奇的神色，看向一旁的芝诺叛军首领。

Messenger: Igor ...
传令兵：伊戈尔……

He mumbles something imperceptible to himself, gradually steadying his breathing.
他用谁也没有注意到的声音喃喃自语，逐渐找回了呼吸的节奏。

And the moment he straightens up, he whips out a pistol.
站直身子的瞬间，他掏出一把手枪。

Messenger: This is for Alyosha!
传令兵：为了……为了阿廖沙！

Igor: Hrgh!
伊戈尔：呵——

Igor staggers back two steps and drops to one knee.
伊戈尔向后踉跄着退了两步，屈膝半跪在了地上。

The messenger's trembling hand throws the shot off course, denying the bullet its deadly purpose.
行刺者颤抖的手让弹道偏离了预定方向，子弹没能忠诚地履行造成死亡的职责。

After a brief moment of shock, the commander lunges forward to seize the messenger's wrist.
短暂的震惊后，军长跨步上前擒住了传令兵的手腕。

Commander Ivan: Soldier! What do you think you're doing?
军长伊万：你在干什么，士兵？

He forces the messenger's face to the ground. He tastes the dirt of his home with every word he roars.
传令兵的脸被军长按在地上，他每咆哮出一个词，都能尝到故乡泥土的腥。

Messenger: Alyosha, my brother. You killed him!
传令兵：我的兄弟——阿廖沙——死了！

Messenger: You killed him in São Paulo!
传令兵：他的血染红了圣保罗！

Messenger: Why did you do it? Because he wasn't an arcanist? Because he wouldn't help you cozy up to the Manus scum?
传令兵：凭什么？因为他不是神秘学家？因为他对你投靠重塑之手没有价值？！

No one answers. Besides his struggling, the night returns to silence.
没有人回答他的话，被枪击声惊扰的夜晚归于寂静。

Commander Ivan: Your commanders may execute any soldier who sabotages the army's strategic goals as a traitor. This is Zeno law.
军长伊万：按照芝诺的军事法，任何蓄意阻碍军队达成战略目标的士兵，将会以叛徒的身份被枪决。

Igor: ...
伊戈尔：……

Igor rises slowly and grips the military identification tag hanging against his chest. A dent remains where the bullet struck.
伊戈尔缓缓站了起来，他握住了坠在胸前的军籍牌，上面留下了被子弹擦过的凹痕。

He is as calm as the silent nights of the Don. He steps forward and kicks the fallen pistol away.
遇刺的叛军领袖的神情沉如顿河静谧的夜晚，他上前一步，踢开了落在地上的作案工具。

Igor: Let him go.
伊戈尔：让他走吧。

Commander Ivan: This is my army. You have no say here.
军长伊万：我的军队不用你来指挥。

Not far off, a few warhorses neigh. Torches quickly light up the dark like streetlights.
不远处传来了战马嘶鸣，一支支火把如街灯亮起。

Messenger: Let go of me!
传令兵：放开我！！

The messenger struggles free of Ivan's grasp, rolls down the slope, and vanishes into the marching column. One by one, the torches go out.
在钳制者分神的瞬间，传令兵挣脱了桎梏向坡下的行军队列中滚去。他很快溜进了行军队伍，火把也逐一灭了。

Commander Ivan: Damn it!
军长伊万：该死的——

The commander fires several shots but hits nothing.
军长对着夜幕开了几枪，但是什么都没有打中。

Commander Ivan: ...
军长伊万：……

Ptolemy: Father!
托勒密：父亲——！

Igor's ever-loyal son rushes over.
忠诚的孩子怨恨自己迟来一步。

Ptolemy: Burakov, don't even think about protecting that bastard.
托勒密：伊万诺维奇，你别想着包庇那个畜——

Igor raises a hand and turns to the commander.
伊戈尔挥手制止了养子未出口的话，转向了军长。

Igor: I'm letting it go, and so are you.
伊戈尔：这件事我可以忘记，你也可以。

Igor: Many of your cavalry have long served Zeno. They will protect him. Pursuing this will only undermine morale.
伊戈尔：你的士兵们在保护他。骑兵军里有不少芝诺的老兵，追究此事容易动摇军心。

Igor: Nothing matters more than unity in the army. I taught you this.
伊戈尔：我告诉过你，在军队中没有什么比团结更重要。

Igor: Your cavalry must be united—brothers and sisters on the battlefield. This is what they are: a family.
伊戈尔：你的骑兵军会是最团结的部队，他们理应像亲人一样关系紧密，相互托付后背。因为他们本来就是亲人。

After a few deep breaths, Ivan returns his pistol to its holster.
在胸口几次深沉的起伏之后，伊万将手枪插回武装带。

Commander Ivan: The hatred of those who once trusted you, a bullet to the chest, Krutov's betrayal, a "home" soon to be aflame ...
军长伊万：怨恨你的幸存者，射向你的子弹，背叛你的格鲁托夫，还有你即将陷入战火的“家”。

Commander Ivan: Remember what you have wrought, Igor.
军长伊万：看看你的所作所为吧，伊戈尔。

Igor: I can never forget.
伊戈尔：我铭记于心。

Igor: About the ritual that caused the Golden Famine.
伊戈尔：……让我们回到没有说完的话，关于那场导致“金色饥荒”的仪式。

Igor: It was Manus Vindictae's doing, composed of multiple rites. Their goal is to expand west. First the Don, then the Dnieper.
伊戈尔：这是重塑之手的杰作，由多重节点术式维系。他们最终希望将仪式扩展到整个顿河与第聂伯河地区。

Igor: Soon, millions of square kilometers of land will grow no crops. All of Europe will face hunger.
伊戈尔：数百万平方公里的黑土地中将没有粮食生长，整个欧洲都会陷入饥饿。

Commander Ivan: What sort of ritualist has the power to change the weather over such an area?
军长伊万：什么样的施术者能用神秘术来影响百万平方公里的气候？

Igor: Remember, Ivan, our enemy is a resurrected "deity."
伊戈尔：不要忘了，伊万，我们的敌人是复生的“神明”。

With Ptolemy's support, Igor makes his way down the slope.
在孩子的搀扶下，叛军领袖走下山坡。

Ritualist: Keep breathing.
仪式术士：现在，保持呼吸。

The ritualist lifts a censer above her head. Colorful fumes spill from its veins.
一只香炉被术士高举过头顶，它的孔隙中涌出色彩迷离的烟雾。

Captive: ...
俘虏：……

They creep around the bound captive like a gaseous myriapod.
烟雾如同一只气态的多足爬行生物一般，在被捆缚的俘虏周身缓缓爬行。

Captive: Urrghh ...
俘虏：呵、呵呃——

Captive: Ahh—
俘虏：啊啊——

Krutov: ...
格鲁托夫：……

Krutov raises an eyebrow of contempt at the horrifying display.
面对这场糟糕的表演，格鲁托夫不屑地挑起眉梢。

Krutov: Is he dead?
格鲁托夫：死了？

The ritualist gives the censer a wave. The fumes rush back inside in an instant.
术士伸手于香炉上方一抚，纷乱的烟雾立时收束。

Ritualist: I overestimated the human's mental capacity. Next time, I won't make the same mistake.
仪式术士：我还是低估了人类精神的孱弱程度，下次应该能把握到更好的分寸。

Krutov: Why not bring them to the Iceberg? Or do you aim to monopolize the fruits of the experiment?
格鲁托夫：为什么不把他们带到“冰山”上去研究？还是说你想的就是独自占有研究的成果？

Ritualist: Every glorious achievement is Hers.
仪式术士：所有的功业都属于祂。

The ritualist's answer is vague. Krutov waves a hand, signaling a soldier to drag the body away.
术士含糊其词地回应着。格鲁托夫挥挥手，让一名士兵将尸体拖走。

Krutov: I have no time for your little experiments. My army departs for Bereggrad in two hours.
格鲁托夫：我没有时间照顾你的实验了，两个小时之后，我的军队就要前往别列格勒。

Ritualist: Bereggrad?
仪式术士：别列格勒？

The news clearly catches her off guard.
她显然没有预料到这个决定。

Ritualist: We will eventually conquer the city—plunder it of its wealth and vitality. But departing now, you say? There were no such orders from the Iceberg.
仪式术士：我们终会夺下那座城市，将它的财富与生命洗劫一空，可是……现在？“冰山”的命令可不是攻取城市。

Ritualist: Starvation will be our enemy's end.
仪式术士：我们会用饥饿来消灭敌人。

Krutov: She entrusted me an army and the power to deploy them. Now that Igor and Ivan have met, they are sure to head to Bereggrad. I must stop them.
格鲁托夫：给了我军队，我就有权力调动他们。伊戈尔和伊万见过面了，他们正在向别列格勒进军，我必须拦住他们。

The low growl of engines rolls in. The transport unit responsible for heavy weapons is departing.
一阵发动机的轰鸣声传来——负责运载重型武器的车队已经启动。

Krutov: My army is ready. This is my chance to eliminate Igor for good.
格鲁托夫：我的军队已经准备好发动一次决战，这是我消灭伊戈尔的最好机会。

Krutov: If you won't give me the "boon," I will take it myself.
格鲁托夫：如果你们不给我那一份奇迹，我就会自己去夺过来。

Ritualist: The loss of one rite will affect the efficacy of many others.
仪式术士：一个节点失守，其他节点的术式范围也会受到影响。

Ritualist: If the weather cools and winter returns, our attack on the Eastern Front will be jeopardized. Our entire project will be delayed.
仪式术士：如果冬天降临，我们接下来在东线的攻势会受到严重影响，而整个针对欧洲的计划都会受到拖延。

Ritualist: Your privilege has its limits, General. You and your followers will accept the trial. It is only a matter of time.
仪式术士：你拥有的特权不是无限的，将军。迟早有一天，你和你的人也要接受祂的试炼。

Krutov: Watch your tone, Ritualist. You never asked Igor and his men to wear the mask. You have no right to ask us.
格鲁托夫：你们没有要求伊戈尔和他的人戴上面具，为什么现在又提出这种要求。

He presses his gun against the ritualist's forehead. But she doesn't flinch. She grins.
一把手枪顶上术士的前额，但后者不为所动，反倒咧嘴而笑。

Krutov: Igor is a weak, indecisive coward who doesn't dare face his true desires. I am greater than he will ever be.
格鲁托夫：伊戈尔只是一个孱弱而优柔寡断的中间派，不敢直面自己的渴望。我比他要强得多。

Ritualist: Do you think a gun to the head will scare me? Death is but a return to Her embrace.
仪式术士：你认为我会害怕死亡吗？祂最终会令所有神秘学家的灵魂复苏，一切功过是非都将得以澄清。

Ritualist: She will resurrect all arcanists—clarify our deeds. I will die with Her name on my lips.
仪式术士：你大可送我去见祂，我将念着祂的美名而死。

Krutov lowers his gun.
格鲁托夫放下枪。

Krutov: Monsters.
格鲁托夫：恶心的怪物。

Charon: ...
卡戎：……

He looks on as one of Krutov's soldiers drags a fresh corpse from the camp.
他看着一名格鲁托夫的士兵将一具新的人类尸体拖至营地外侧。

For the man who is already dead, this moment feels closer to withering.
对于已死之人来说，此刻的感受更加接近于“枯萎”。

Charon: How many more will die for this?
卡戎：还会有多少人因此而死？

Manus Soldier: ...
重塑士兵：……

The soldier glances at him. Without a word, he lowers his head and walks away. Charon feels a stronger pressure closing in on him.
士兵乜斜一眼，没有回话，低着头离开了。卡戎感受到一种更强的压迫感正在靠近。

Krutov: They die because of you, Charon. The decision was yours.
格鲁托夫：这是你自己选的，卡戎，他们的死都会算在你头上。

Charon turns back.
亡者回过头。

Charon: If joining you will teach you to respect life, then, I will do so.
卡戎：如果我的顺从能换来你对生命的敬重，我不会拒绝。

Charon: But it is unclear what difference my choice will make. These people will still die by your hand, only for another cause.
卡戎：可是……我不理解我的选择能带来多少改变，这些人仍然会因为其他的理由死在你的手上。

Krutov: I am not so deranged as the Manus. I simply value efficiency.
格鲁托夫：我没有重塑之手那么疯狂，我只是喜欢效率。

Charon: If you are not one of them, if you detest their behavior, why do you work for them?
卡戎：既然你厌恶重塑之手的行径，也并不属于戴上面具的那些人，那为什么要为他们服务？

Krutov: They work for me. These lunatics know nothing of military tactics.
格鲁托夫：是他们在为我服务，因为这群神秘学家疯子需要我，不然他们根本找不到懂得军事战略的人。

Krutov: They simply point in a direction and let their entire force loose.
格鲁托夫：他们所谓的将领，只会愚蠢地让所有能用的士兵往一个方向进攻。

Krutov pauses.
格鲁托夫顿了顿。

Krutov: What similarity could I possibly have with them?
格鲁托夫：你觉得我和他们有什么相似点？

Charon: You both start wars, bringing destruction and bloodshed wherever you walk.
卡戎：战争，破坏和无止尽的死亡。

Krutov: *scoff* Our purposes are different.
格鲁托夫：目的是不同的。

Charon: It doesn't seem so different to me.
卡戎：对我来说没有什么不同。

Krutov: A pity you have to go. It's rare I meet someone worth more than a few words of conversation.
格鲁托夫：可惜，如果你能留在我身边，会是一个好的聊天对象。

Charon fails to grasp the implication behind Krutov's words. He only sees a soldier approaching with a black horse.
亡者未能及时领悟到格鲁托夫话中的暗示。他看见一名士兵牵着一匹黑马向自己走来。

Charon: Andreas?
卡戎：安德烈森？

Andreas: Neigh ...
安德烈森：咴……

Charon: ...?
卡戎：……？

Before he even realizes it, he is bound with rope by the two soldiers behind him.
在他没察觉到的时候，已经有两名士兵来到身后，此刻正用粗硬的草绳捆缚他的双手。

Charon: Why do you bind me? The ritualist has already limited my movement.
卡戎：这是在做什么？你们已经用术阵限制了我的行动。

Krutov: I have other plans for you.
格鲁托夫：我对你有别的安排。

19  时代遗物 / Legacy of the Era

Legacy of the Era
时代遗物

Street, Bereggrad
别列格勒·街道

Bereggrad receives the thundering hooves of the cavalry in dead silence. It's as if the city has been deserted.
在军队的马蹄踏入别列格勒时，城市用死一样的寂静迎接骑兵军的到来。它看起来就像是一座空城。

Yet the soldiers sense the stares—frightened, alert, resentful—peering through every rusted doorway, every shattered stained-glass window. They settle on the weary warhorses, on dust-stained uniforms, and on the black barrels of their guns.
但士兵们都能感受到那种目光——惊恐、警惕而怨恨的——从每一扇锈腐的门后，从修道院破碎的彩窗后面，打在疲惫的战马、落灰的制服和黑洞洞的枪管上。

Sternova: What are they so scared of? The cavalry's here to protect them.
斯捷尔诺娃：为什么别列格勒的人对骑兵军会是这个态度？

Commander Ivan: After all the chaos—letters from the front, soaring prices, bandits, Bereggrad is worn thin, even afraid of its own shadow.
军长伊万：别列格勒目前没有被任何一方完全控制，但混乱从来没有停止：送回家乡的讣告、飞涨的物价、走上劫掠道路的年轻人。这片草原没有一块土地是完好的，人也是。

Sternova: *sigh* Just like the people of Tamarovka.
斯捷尔诺娃：这倒让我想起……在红柳树村的时候也是差不多的状况。

The commander looks up at the shuttered windows around him, then turns to the shadows hiding in the alleys.
军长抬头看着所有紧闭的门窗，又转向在巷道中闪躲的影子。

Igor: You didn't keep the cavalry's orders confidential?
伊戈尔：你没有在军队中严格保密骑兵军接到的命令？

Igor walks beside him. From time to time, he presses against his ribs, where the sting of the gunshot still echoes.
伊戈尔走在军长身旁，他时不时用手摁住肋部——那里还留着昨夜刺杀事件的枪伤。

Commander Ivan: Word always gets out.
军长伊万：这种事情很难不传出去。

Igor: Then the citizens must know that you will soon withdraw from the Don. With no one left to protect them, they have lost all faith.
伊戈尔：那别列格勒的市民应该也知道骑兵军马上就会离开顿河。他们对局面失去了信心，因为没有人会保护他们。

Igor: They do not know why the cavalry comes. I'm certain their minds are filled with the worst possibilities.
伊戈尔：但他们不理解为什么骑兵军在离开之前还要来别列格勒，只能往最坏的方向去推测。

Igor: They think you will plunder the city, no doubt. That's most of what they have faced since this began.
伊戈尔：他们会猜测，骑兵军是不是要在走之前将别列格勒洗劫一空。毕竟自从内乱开始以来，劫掠就是草原的常态。

Commander Ivan: I see. I'll find a man of standing. Fyodor Petrovich, perhaps. And convince him we bear no ill.
军长伊万：我会去找几个有声望的人，比如费奥多尔·彼得罗维奇，一位市民代表。我会让他相信骑兵军没有恶意。

Igor: People don't understand what they cannot see with their own eyes, and you cannot tell them about the Foundation or Zeno.
伊戈尔：人们不会理解自己看不到的东西，你没办法把基金会和芝诺的事情解释给他们听。

In the darkness, several citizens lurk with weapons in their hands. Not one of them escapes Igor's eyes.
阴影处潜伏着一些手持武器的市民，这一切无法躲过军人敏锐的目光。

Igor: They have already formed a militia, preparing for the worst. All we can do is remain discreet and avoid adding fuel to the fire.
伊戈尔：看来他们已经组织起了民兵，做好了最坏的打算。我们能做的只有在城里保持安静，不要再火上浇油。

Commander Ivan: We must end this quickly. Deal with Krutov. Captured or killed, it doesn't matter. Destroy their ritual, and return the steppe to what she once was.
军长伊万：……我只希望这一切快点结束。解决格鲁托夫，最好逮捕或杀死他本人，然后毁坏正在控制顿河天气的术阵。

Igor: And aid the Timekeeper in her mission.
伊戈尔：以及让司辰完成基金会交给她的任务。

Commander Ivan: One week, Igor. That was your word. Do not forget it.
军长伊万：没错，所有这一切——一星期之内完成，你答应过我。也希望你能信守承诺。

Igor: I suggest we entrust the ritual issue to Lilya's flight unit.
伊戈尔：嗯，我建议将解决术阵的问题交给红弩箭的飞行中队。

Igor: They have the element of surprise. An aerial strike will give the Manus little time to react.
伊戈尔：她们发动奇袭的效果比骑兵更好，可以在重塑之手反应过来之前破坏仪式。

Commander Ivan: Already done. The coordinates you gave are with Lieutenant Lilya. She's working on an air raid plan as we speak.
军长伊万：我也是这么计划的，你提供的地点我已经转交给红弩箭中尉，她正在制订空袭计划。

Igor: I will leave Bereggrad to you. The Timekeeper waits for me at the church.
伊戈尔：别列格勒就暂时交给你了。我该去见司辰了，她正在修道院等我。

Commander Ivan: What she seeks is in a church?
军长伊万：你们要调查的线索，在修道院里？

Lilya brings her broom to a stop outside a church.
红弩箭的扫帚停在一座修道院前。

This church is unremarkable among the many others in Bereggrad—medium-sized and without a distinctive dome. Only its position sets it apart. It stands on higher ground, giving it a clear overlook of the golden plains.
在别列格勒的无数修道院中，这实在是不起眼的一座，不大也不小，并不特殊的圆顶，只是地势稍稍高了一些，可以远远望见深秋金色的原野。

Lilya: What kind of church is this? Place is abandoned.
红弩箭：破修道院，连修士都跑了。

Lilya: Captain, I don't know what you're looking for here, but you got to take care of yourself, especially ...
红弩箭：头儿，我不知道你想来这儿找什么——但你得万事小心，特别是……

As she glances over, she spots Igor approaching from afar.
她斜睨了一眼，伊戈尔正从远处向修道院走来。

Vertin: I will, Lilya. Have a good flight.
维尔汀：我会的，红弩箭，也祝你的飞行一切顺利。

Lilya: Never had a bad one.
红弩箭：对我来说，没有不顺利的飞行。

Lilya: Though smashing into the vice president's office would make it a little better than the rest.
红弩箭：虽然我有时候也希望自己能失手砸中副会长办公室。

It's a farewell in Lilya's own style. But she doesn't leave. Instead, she fixes her gaze on Igor.
这是红弩箭式的告别，但飞行员并没有离开，她意有所指地看向另一边的伊戈尔。

Igor: Lieutenant Lilya, my interests do not conflict with the Timekeeper's. The coordinates I provided are correct. We're on the same side.
伊戈尔：红弩箭中尉，我与司辰小队间没有利益冲突。交给你的术阵节点坐标也是真实的，你大可放心。

Lilya: I am not worried. If you dare to lie about this, you're not leaving the city alive.
红弩箭：我并不担心这个，如果你在这件事上说谎，那你多半也走不出别列格勒了。

Lilya steps forward, directly squaring up with the rebel leader.
红弩箭朝前走了两步，直直站在叛军将领的正前方。

Lilya: But no matter what you do, now or in the future, I'll never forget what you did in São Paulo.
红弩箭：我想说的是，不管你现在做了什么，将来还会做什么，我都不会忘记你在圣保罗做的事。

Lilya: I'll live through this war, and I hope you do too, so I can walk you to the judgment seat myself and see you punished for everything you've done.
红弩箭：我会亲手把你押送到审判席上，让你面对每一条罪行。我一定会活到战争结束，我也希望你能活到那一天。

She meets his eyes head-on, like an eagle locking onto its prey.
飞行女巫盯住对方的双眼，像鹰爪死死地抓住猎物。

Lilya: And if the court doesn't sentence you to death, I will.
红弩箭：如果法庭不会判你死刑，那我会。

Meeting Lilya's eyes, Igor understands that this isn't a threat but a declaration.
伊戈尔看着红弩箭的眼睛，明白她的话并非威胁，只是宣告。

He doesn't confront her, but he doesn't avoid her gaze either.
他没有迎击，也没有躲避。

Igor: Lieutenant, I know what I've done. I see the faces of those I sacrificed more clearly than anyone.
伊戈尔：中尉，我记得我做过的每一件事，我记得我牺牲过的每一个人，我比任何一位法官都记得更清楚，所以——

A suffocating stillness settles across his face.
他的脸上浮现出一种被压缩为宁静的苦闷神情。

Igor: I look forward to my judgment day.
伊戈尔：我很期待那一天。

Lilya: So do I.
红弩箭：我也一样。

Lilya mounts her broom and takes off. Igor watches as she rises into the sky and rejoins her squad.
飞行女巫骑上扫帚，离开了地面。伊戈尔目送她升入高空，与中队的其他飞行员会合。

He lowers his head.
将军低下头。

Igor: Let's go, Timekeeper. What you search for is underneath the church.
伊戈尔：司辰，走吧，你要找的东西就在修道院之下。

Vertin: Underneath?
维尔汀：修道院的……下面？

Igor: As eras change, so do landmarks. There was a time the Amber Room was buried beneath a market, even.
伊戈尔：历史变迁会让地标建筑的位置发生改变，琥珀屋过去甚至有过深埋于农业市场地下的时期。

Igor: The church's cellar is deep, near the periphery of the Amber Room. Ptolemy led the digging of a simple tunnel between them.
伊戈尔：现在这个修道院的地窖很深，刚好与琥珀屋的外围空间相隔不远。我已经让托勒密带人开凿了一条通向目的地的简易隧道。

Just as they are about to set off, Sonetto lets out her anxious hesitation.
在即将出发的时刻，身后还是响起了第一助手焦虑不安的迟疑。

Sonetto: Timekeeper, are you sure you want to go down there alone?
十四行诗：司辰……您真的要一个人下去吗？

Vertin: It's alright, Sonetto. Only Igor and I are allowed in the Amber Room. That was the deal.
维尔汀：不用担心，十四行诗，只有我和伊戈尔能进入琥珀屋，这是之前说好的。

Vertin: You and Marsha can just wait for me here.
维尔汀：你和玛尔纱在地面等我出来就好。

The road down is not long, but the darkness of the suffocating earth devours any sense of time.
向下的道路并不漫长，只是黑暗与厚重沉闷的泥土吞食了时间感。

Igor: ...
伊戈尔：……

Guided by the lingering scent of gunpowder from Ptolemy's excavation, they move toward a faint point of light in the distance.
循着爆破后残留的火药味，他们向黑暗尽头的一处光斑前进。

Vertin: What's that light ahead?
维尔汀：前面的光亮是什么？

Igor: The lighting system my men built before.
伊戈尔：是我的人之前在内部建立的照明系统。

Vertin: Before? When they dug the tunnel?
维尔汀：之前？挖通隧道的时候吗？

Igor: Earlier than that.
伊戈尔：是比那更早的事了。

The light grows brighter, revealing an unexpectedly complex structure as they pass through the entryway.
那一束光线越来越张扬，些许复杂的结构逐渐显现，直到探索者越过那一道门。

Vertin: ...!!
维尔汀：——！

Metro Station, Bereggrad
别列格勒·地铁站

It appears to be a vast subway station or some grand theatrical stage.
面前的大厅像是一个巨大的地铁站，又像是上演戏剧的辉煌舞台。

Whether or not an ode has ever been sung here, this is clearly not something that could have been built in this era.
无论是否有人曾在这里低吟颂歌，这儿都显然不是属于这个时代的宏伟工程。

Vertin: A metro station. Looks like Cold War period. What's it doing here?
维尔汀：这是一座冷战时期风格的地铁站，为什么会出现在这里？

Igor: It's because of the Amber Room. The range of its "Storm" immunity expanded, covering this station.
伊戈尔：是琥珀屋的作用。它对“暴雨”免疫的能力稍微向四周蔓延，就在历史的不断回溯中留下了这一截地铁站。

Vertin: The immunity range expanded. So this isn't the center of the Amber Room?
维尔汀：免疫“暴雨”能力的蔓延……这里还不是琥珀屋的核心？

Igor: Of course not. There was a metro station here when the Foundation and Zeno built the laboratory.
伊戈尔：当然不是，在基金会与芝诺联手在琥珀屋建立实验室的时候，它的上方就已经存在着一座真正的、为市民提供服务的地铁站。

Vertin: A laboratory built in collaboration between the Foundation and Zeno?
维尔汀：基金会与芝诺联手建造的实验室？

Igor: There is still some distance to the Amber Room. Let's talk on the way. Much remains to explain.
伊戈尔：……抵达琥珀屋的入口还有些距离，让我们在路上谈谈吧，司辰。我的确有很多要解释的事。

Now they've entered the subway station. The aged Zeno traitor finally seems to shed the veiled wording in his speech.
进入地铁站之后，年迈的芝诺背叛者似乎终于卸下了所有言辞中的遮掩。

Igor: The Foundation and Zeno have worked on many projects together of many security levels. I am now free to talk of them.
伊戈尔：基金会和芝诺联手开展过很多项目，涉及不同的保密等级，当然，这些保密规定已经不再对我有约束力。

Igor: After the second "Storm," several Zeno generals and Committee members requested my military support for a research project on the "Storm."
伊戈尔：在第二次“暴雨”之后，几个芝诺高级将领与基金会委员找到我，希望我为一项涉及“暴雨”的研究提供军事援助。

Igor: At first, I thought it was similar to the Changeling project. Shady, perhaps, but not too harmful.
伊戈尔：最初，我以为这只是一个类似于“换生灵”的研究项目，虽然见不得光，但终究算不上会带来太大危害。

Vertin: That project still caused harm to tourists at the Green Lake Campsite and the surrounding residents.
维尔汀：“换生灵”项目还是给绿湖营地的游客，以及周边的居民带来了伤害。

Igor: This is nothing compared to what the Amber Room did.
伊戈尔：和琥珀屋比起来，这些又很难称得上是大问题。

Igor: After I joined the project, my orders were to station on the outskirts of Bereggrad and build a military zone to protect the underground site.
伊戈尔：我答应参与之后，得到进驻别列格勒近郊，在地面上建立军事警戒区，保护地下设施不受侵入的命令。

Igor: At the same time, the Foundation asked for a thorough review of all soldiers who served Zeno after the first "Storm" but did not appear in any 1999 records at headquarters.
伊戈尔：同时基金会要求我排查那些在“暴雨”后各时代中为芝诺服役，却不存在于1999年芝诺总部记录中的士兵。

Vertin: Were they not registered?
维尔汀：他们是没有登记的人吗？

Igor: No. Their records were flawless in each branch across the world. But compared to headquarters' records from 1999, anomalies were found.
伊戈尔：不。这些士兵在芝诺各分部都保有完整可靠的档案，可是将分部档案与1999年的总部记录对比后，就会发现异常。

Igor: Some had similar resumes and ranks but different photos from those filed in headquarters, as if their identities were stolen or replaced.
伊戈尔：一样的军衔、相似的履历，但照片却和总部保存的图像资料完全不同，如同被另一个人顶替了身份。

Igor: Zeno also encountered people whose faces matched the archive but whose names were different, with no connection to Zeno at all.
伊戈尔：而另一边，芝诺又偶尔会在不同时代中发现一些相貌与总部档案中的完全一致，却在当下与芝诺毫无瓜葛的人。

Igor: Sound familiar, Timekeeper?
伊戈尔：这些事，听起来会让你感到熟悉吗，司辰？

Vertin: Yes, Mr. Duncan in São Paulo. His appearance matched an acquaintance of mine, a man I saw dissolve in the "Storm."
维尔汀：是的，圣保罗的邓肯先生。他的外貌与曾经消失在“暴雨”中的一位故人十分相似。

Igor: I was tasked with locating these abnormalities and bringing them to the Amber Room. For what purpose, I did not know.
伊戈尔：我替琥珀屋项目找到这些异常的人，带到琥珀屋，却不知道在地下发生了什么。

Igor: The guard there confided to me that people looked different after leaving, as if no longer themselves.
伊戈尔：管理出入的士兵曾私下告诉我，那些被送出去的人，和他们被送进来时的神色完全不同，像是被改变了心智。

Vertin: Did you ever consider finding out what was going on?
维尔汀：你想过弄清楚地下发生的事吗？

Igor: No. Back then, my faith was firm. I wholeheartedly believed in the Foundation's vision of peace and honored my duty. That is to say, I followed orders.
伊戈尔：没有。那时我对我们的信念坚如磐石，我深信基金会许诺的和平愿景，更以恪守军人的天职——服从为荣耀。

Igor: That led to my deepest regret. After the sixth "Storm," they brought in Urd.
伊戈尔：但这就是令我后悔的事……在第六次“暴雨”之后，他们带来了兀尔德。

Vertin: ...?
维尔汀：——？

Vertin: The Foundation found Urd that long ago?
维尔汀：在那么早之前，基金会就已经找到兀尔德了吗？

Igor: Specifically, those leading the Amber Room Project did. By order of the Pax House, all information on the project was sealed.
伊戈尔：确切来说，是主导琥珀屋项目的人找到了兀尔德。但在鸽子屋的要求下，他们对项目外的人封锁了信息。

Igor: The two subsequent "Storms" came suddenly. During that time, something happened to Urd, something different.
伊戈尔：之后的两场“暴雨”来得太突然，而兀尔德进入琥珀屋后，她的状态也出现了异常。

Igor: During one "Storm," she escaped, but the secrecy of the project left us without manpower to find her.
伊戈尔：她在“暴雨”持续期间设法脱离了琥珀屋的控制，但碍于保密指令，当时我们并没有足够的人手去寻找她。

Igor: This is what led to her multiple disappearances in the following "Storms."
伊戈尔：这件事间接导致了她在后续“暴雨”中的多次失踪。

Vertin: She could've stayed in the safe zone, couldn't she?
维尔汀：……她本来可以留在安全区域内的，是吗？

Igor: Indeed. From my view, the Foundation only searched for her to cover for their mistakes.
伊戈尔：对。在我的视角看来，之后基金会对兀尔德的搜寻都是在掩饰自己的失误。

Igor: I remember well the last time I saw Urd. It was on a vast land, dangerous, barren, much like the Don steppe.
伊戈尔：我还记得最后一次见到兀尔德时的情境，和顿河的草原一样，那里也是一片危险而荒芜的旷野。

Vertin: In Antarctica?
维尔汀：在南极？

Igor: That was Doctor Dores. I talk of Urd.
伊戈尔：不，在南极的是德洛丝医生，而我说的是兀尔德。

Igor: When I saw her in São Paulo, I knew her at once. Both by her appearance and by her unique calm bravery.
伊戈尔：在圣保罗见到她的时候，虽然样貌没有变化，而且仍然有她特有的平静的勇气……

Igor: But Doctor Dores was not the woman I knew in 1999. She was lost. A soul trying to reclaim memory and identity.
伊戈尔：但德洛丝医生终究和1999年的兀尔德判若两人。德洛丝医生茫然地想要找回自己的身份，早已忘记自己是谁。

Vertin: In other words, the last time you saw the Urd you knew was before the first "Storm."
维尔汀：……也就是说，你最后一次见到你口中的兀尔德，是在第一次“暴雨”来临之前。

Igor: Correct. In 1999, the year the first "Storm" rose rain from the earth. This is the second event I want to tell you about.
伊戈尔：1999年，我，还有很多人，都见证了第一次“暴雨”的降临。这是我想说的第二件往事。

Igor: At that time, our army stood ready in Kazakhstan for a war none had ever seen before.
伊戈尔：那时候我们的军队在哈萨克斯坦严阵以待，准备一场史无前例的战役……

Igor: Ms. Urd, the army is combat ready.
伊戈尔：兀尔德女士，军队全员已经做好战斗准备了。

Urd: Thank you for letting me know, Admiral. At the moment they start the ritual, they'll be their most vulnerable. I need to be close to its center at that time.
兀尔德：麻烦您了，将军。仪式举行的瞬间，会是他们最脆弱的时候，我需要在那个瞬间靠近仪式中心。

Igor: Understood. You know what you're doing. I believe in you.
伊戈尔：好。我相信您知道自己在做什么。

Igor: Their defenses are weak. There's no need to worry, Ms. Urd. Everything will go smoothly.
伊戈尔：重塑之手的防御很薄弱，行动会很顺利，请您不用担心，兀尔德女士。

Urd: I'm not worried about myself. I'm worried about her.
兀尔德：我不为自己的命运担忧，不过，我很担心她。

Igor: You mean your daughter?
伊戈尔：她……您的孩子，对吗？

Urd: Yes. She's still too young to understand all this.
兀尔德：是的，她还太小，还不能真正理解身边正在发生的事。

Igor: The SPDM will take good care of her.
伊戈尔：第一防线学校会照看好她的。

Urd: The Foundation made the same promise. I suppose it's the only way.
兀尔德：基金会给了我一样的承诺……我想也只能如此了。

Vertin: Manus Vindictae held its first space-time ritual on December 31st, 1999.
维尔汀：……1999年12月31日，重塑之手举行了第一次时空仪式。

Igor: If they had succeeded, that ritual would have brought time back to the Blank Era. Antarctica was their second attempt.
伊戈尔：时空仪式一旦成功，就能让重塑之手回到属于神秘学家的“空白时代”。南极是他们的第二次尝试。

Igor: The results were much the same. The "Flood" summoned by the ritual was incomplete and turned into the first "Storm."
伊戈尔：两次时空仪式的结局都是两败俱伤，仪式发挥了效用，但没有彻底完成。“洪水”化作“暴雨”顷刻而至。

Footsteps echo through the long-sealed subway station, drawing them ever closer to the heart of the secret.
足音回响在这座封存已久的地铁站里，正一步步靠近秘密的核心。

Igor: I promised Urd the operation would go smoothly, but I lied. Neither Zeno nor the Foundation had any idea how powerful the Manus's defensive ritual was.
伊戈尔：我向兀尔德保证行动会顺利，但这是谎言。芝诺和基金会都不清楚重塑之手布设的防御术阵有多么强大。

Igor: The situation was desperate. We had only one order: advance, regardless of the casualties.
伊戈尔：情况过于危急，所有战斗人员都只得到了一条命令——前进，不计伤亡。

Igor: When the "Storm" rose, it swallowed the details of the war. Yet the weight of it haunts me still—the grief of sending my soldiers to die.
伊戈尔：战役的细节被“暴雨”带走，只剩沉重的情绪还留在我的回忆中，那是下令让无数士兵走向死亡的悲痛。

Igor: Like the flame of a candle, they were extinguished in an instant.
伊戈尔：无数性命像酒杯口的小虫子一样，轻易地滑向了深渊，再也没有回来。

Igor: If it weren't for the "Storm" blurring my memories, I would've left Zeno and the Foundation far sooner.
伊戈尔：如果不是被第一场“暴雨”或多或少带走了记忆，我离开芝诺与基金会的时间只会更早。

The subway station ends, and ahead of them lies a broader space, where white light and air pour in.
地铁站走到了尽头，前面似乎是一个更广阔的地带，白色的光线和风灌了进来。

Vertin: Then, what ultimately led to your betrayal?
维尔汀：那之后又是什么导致了你背叛芝诺和基金会？

Igor halts and turns toward an obscure door at the corner of the hall.
将军停下了脚步，看向大厅角落不起眼的门。

Igor: We've arrived.
伊戈尔：——我们到了。

It's carved from the same smooth marble as the surrounding wall, its outline only perceptible on close inspection. Unobtrusive, unremarkable even, but ever so mysterious.
它和墙壁是一样光滑的大理石，需要仔细看才能辨认出轮廓。但从视线停在门上的那一刻起，所有人都感觉得到其神秘。

Igor: It was the Amber Room Project. It showed me the Pax House's arrogance.
伊戈尔：……正是琥珀屋项目让我想起了鸽子屋治下一切的傲慢。

Igor: By now, I don't know which shows less respect for life, the Foundation or Manus Vindictae.
伊戈尔：我不清楚，在对生命的漠视方面，基金会和重塑之手究竟谁更加令人绝望。

Igor: I once thought that we fought and sacrificed for a firm conviction, to bring a bright and peaceful future.
伊戈尔：我曾以为，我们的战斗和牺牲都建立在充足的理由与信念上，都是为了一个和平而美好的未来。

Igor: But I can fool myself no longer. The Pax House may aim to build a peaceful future for both humans and arcanists, but their means are no longer justifiable.
伊戈尔：但在见过这一切之后，我很难再欺骗自己。我知道鸽子屋在缔造属于人类与神秘学家共同的和平，但我已不再相信他们的路径。

Igor: And, with Arcana as She is, this is a war for gods. How can we stand—let alone claim victory—as mere mortals?
伊戈尔：而且……阿尔卡纳的存在让我意识到，这是一场属于神明的战争，作为凡人，我不知道如何取胜。

Igor: How I wish to have a god on my side, Timekeeper.
伊戈尔：我希望，也有一位神明能站在自己这边，司辰。

There is an almost pleading note in his voice.
他的态度几近恳求。

Igor: What lies in the Amber Room, I cannot fully say. Data fragments, Ms. Urd's words, whatever it is, she wants you to have it.
伊戈尔：或许是实验的全部资料，或许只是兀尔德女士留给你的只言片语。我不知道这里面具体有什么，但我知道这是她希望留给你的东西。

Igor: Destiny has seen it survive several "Storms," waiting for this hour, for you.
伊戈尔：因“命运”的力量，它们得以在“暴雨”中长存，只是为了等待你的到来。我想，你应该接受它。

Igor: This is a door only you and Ms. Urd may open. Go. Walk through it.
伊戈尔：穿过这扇门。这是只有你与兀尔德女士能打开的门。

Vertin: I'm still not sure if you're telling the truth, but thank you for bringing me here, Igor.
维尔汀：……我不能确定你说的一切是否属实，不过，谢谢你能带我到这里，伊戈尔。

20  云雀何处安栖 / Where Does the Skylark Rest

Look at these boys. They left home with puffed chests and confident smiles on their faces—
？？？：看看这群人，他们走出家门时心中满是荣誉感，脸上也都是自信的笑容。

Thinking they'd go to the front, pull a few triggers, kill a few enemies, and come back as heroes.
？？？：脑子里想着到战场上活动活动食指，杀死几个敌人，成为国家英雄。

But when their feet hit the soil, they turned "pacifist," it seems, wounding their own hands rather than their enemies'.
？？？：却突然在前线善心大发，开始呼吁起和平，宁愿伤害自己的手也不愿伤害敌人。

Their mothers should have begged them to stay, not waved their handkerchiefs to see them off.
？？？：这群蠢材的母亲当时就该哭着抱住他们，叫他们不要去，而不是挥着手帕目送他们离开。

选项 / Choices

1. They made a misjudgment.
1. 他们只是一时糊涂

2. They had no choice.
2. 有时人们别无选择

分支 1 / Branch 1

Ha, "misjudgment"—quite the convenient excuse.
？？？：每个人都只是一时糊涂。

Soldiers follow orders, officers give them, higher-ups make them, parents send their children off to die ...
？？？：上前线的士兵，下命令的军官，做决定的贵族，送孩子去死的家长。

And not one of them will bear the weight of their "misjudgments." Only the dead must answer.
？？？：谁都不愿意承担犯糊涂的后果，那么这群家伙就只能遭这报应了。

分支 2 / Branch 2

Really? You think death is the only option?
？？？：那请告诉我唯一的选择是什么？死亡？

What could possibly be the point in that?
？？？：死在这里有什么意义？

Don't tell me you believe there's GLORY in dying on the battlefield? The thought makes me sick.
？？？：别告诉我死在战场上是什么伟大的牺牲，我都快吐了。

Look there—at the lifeless body lying flat in the mud.
？？？：最后一切都落在了外面那个可怜虫头上。

He couldn't even muster the courage to flee, so he bribed a fellow soldier—a man he barely knew—with three cigarettes to surrender with him.
？？？：他甚至鼓不起勇气自己逃跑，所以用三根烟和同一个战壕里的陌生人交了朋友。

He died—killed in a moment of pure, senseless hatred. And the other lived—saved by a flicker of mercy.
？？？：结果他死了，因为前一秒的仇恨，而他的朋友暂时活了下来，因为下一秒诞生的对生命的尊重。

Where should his mother aim her grief? At the officer who changed his mind just a moment too late? Hah!
？？？：他的母亲该把这股愤怒发泄给谁？那个突然悔悟的军官？

The officer will be pardoned; they'll say he, too, was a victim of this endless, miserable cycle of hatred.
？？？：毫无可能，人们会谅解那军官，因为他也是无止境的仇恨的受害者。

So tell me—please, tell me—to whom this rage should go, and I will take this kitchen knife and plunge it into their heart!
？？？：现在请告诉我吧，请告诉我这股仇恨究竟该流向谁，我会用这把厨刀去捅它。

共同接续 / Shared continuation

Excerpt from the New York Times
《纽约新闻报》节选

Around midnight two days ago, a large fire broke out in the oak forest on the western outskirts. The delay of the warning and the complex terrains full of inflammables in the forest resulted in a fire that lasted a whole night till dawn and 13 deaths.
The victims entered the forest at dusk. Identities of 6 of them have been confirmed: the Weyerhaeusers, their buyers Tomson Joyce, Shelley Gerald, Kelly Keith, and the project manager Russell James. The others' are still being verified.

The Weyerhaeuser used to be a leader in the wood industry, yet it went bankrupt not long ago due to the wood supply crisis these years. After donating the formula of the Resurrection Potion (basic version) to the Maria Sanatorium in New York, the Sacred Heart Sanatorium in Ohio, and the Presbyterian Church Health Center on certain conditions, the Weyerhaeusers sold all of their forestry lands.
On the night of the fire, the Weyerhaeusers, their buyers, and the workers went into the forest to start the disafforestation project before they died. The police has found traces of arcanist's activities and a small amount of gasoline at the scene, which shows the case is an arson attack. More investigations are needed to identify the suspect.

Reporter: James Welles/Dorey Johnson
前日深夜，位于市郊西侧的橡木树林发生大范围火灾，由于预警不及，林中地形复杂且可燃物繁多，抢险困难，火势持续直至清晨时分才得以扑灭。
     于当日黄昏时分进入森林的十三名人员全部遇难。死亡人员中已确认身份的六人，包括韦耶豪瑟夫妇，与其交易者汤姆森·乔伊斯、雪莱·杰拉德、凯利·基思，施工负责人罗素·詹姆斯。其余人员身份均在进一步核实中。

     因近年来的木材供应风波，原本根基深重的韦耶豪瑟在前些日子宣告破产。韦耶豪瑟夫妇将基础版起死回生剂的配方，有偿捐赠与纽约玛利亚疗养院、俄亥俄州圣心疗养所与长老会医疗中心三方后，又将名下的所有林业用地对外抛售。
     火情发生当晚，韦耶豪瑟夫妇与购买者三人带领工作小组进入森林，预备开始铲除作业，随后遇难。经市警署排查与分析，火情现场的残留有神秘学家活动与少量汽油的痕迹，已可确定为人为纵火案件。更多关于犯罪嫌疑人的信息仍在进一步调查当中。

     报道记者：詹姆斯·威尔斯/多丽·约翰逊

21  赫克托耳宣言 / The Defender's Oath

The Defender's Oath
赫克托耳宣言

Laboratory Technician: How are you feeling, Ms. Urd?
实验人员：兀尔德女士，你感觉如何？

Urd?: Just as I have the many other times I've sat on this chair. And must you insist on calling me by my pen name?
兀尔德？：和之前的每一次一样，这张椅子我已经坐上去过很多次了。另外，你们一定要用我的笔名来称呼我吗？

Laboratory Technician: Urd is your real name. If you want to find the true you, you need to accept it.
实验人员：那才是属于你的名字。你想要找回你自己，那首先就要接受它。

Urd?: But I'm just a journalist, an occasional writer on hippies. Urd is simply a pseudonym for my submissions to a certain magazine.
兀尔德？：我只是一名调查记者，偶尔给一些嬉皮士写一写传记。“兀尔德”只是我用来给特定杂志投稿的笔名。

Urd?: I still don't understand the purpose of this experiment. Your claims of "world peace" sound no different from many of my interviewees on the street.
兀尔德？：我不知道这些实验的意义，你们口中那些“为了世界和平”的话，倒很像我的一些在街头生活的客户……

Laboratory Technician: She's ready.
实验人员：她已经准备好了。

Laboratory Technician: Vessel ensoulment test number 13, begin.
实验人员：那么，第13次灌体实验，开始——

Urd: Vertin?
兀尔德：——维尔汀？

Vertin: Mum?
维尔汀：……妈妈？

Urd: It really is you, my child.
兀尔德：真的是你，孩子……

Urd: It's been so, so very long. You're slighter than I'd imagined, but how wonderful it is to see how you've grown.
兀尔德：好久没有见面了，你的样子比我预想中的要瘦弱许多。但你长大了，这真的太好了。

Vertin: Why are you here? This isn't reality. I can feel it. Where are you really? Where can I find you?
维尔汀：为什么你会在这里？这不是现实，我能感觉得到……你在哪里？我之后要去哪里才能找到你？

Urd: Don't cry, my child.
兀尔德：不要流泪，孩子……

Urd: This is but a moment from the past. I can only tell you what has already happened.
兀尔德：此刻只是过往的一瞬，我只能告诉你一些有关过去的事——

???: Was the ensoulment successful?
？？？：灌体实验成功了？

Laboratory Technician: Not quite, but it's the closest we've ever gotten. Amazing, all previous subjects couldn't bear the side effects of the experiment, only Urd—
实验人员：接近成功。太令人惊叹了，之前所有的受试个体，全都无法承受实验的副作用，只有兀尔德——

???: State the conclusion.
？？？：说结论。

Laboratory Technician: Yes, sir. Urd's true personality may have briefly returned during the 13th test.
实验人员：——是。在第13次实验时，兀尔德的人格也许曾在一瞬间回归。

Laboratory Technician: We know this because of the disappearance of the Amber Room. That is to say, for a few seconds, the Room became unobservable. All feedback signals were cut off.
实验人员：证据是琥珀屋突然消失了。换一个说法，琥珀屋突然变得无法观测，视觉和其他反馈信号全部中断。

Laboratory Technician: My assumption is that the Amber Room traveled into the future during that time.
实验人员：这种情况持续了数秒。我猜，是兀尔德借用这一瞬间的回归，让琥珀屋前往了未来。

???: The future? Why? For what?
？？？：未来？——她要去未来做什么？

...
……

???: Only time will tell.
？？？：那就只有在未来才知道了。

...
……

???: Timekeeper.
？？？：司辰……

???: Timekeeper?
？？？：司辰？

Vertin: Er ...
维尔汀：呃嗯……

???: Timekeeper! Are you awake?
？？？：——司辰！您醒了吗？

Whether it's the ground beneath her or herself that is shaking, she can't tell. With the clamor of noises around her, it's as though she's just been thrown into a hurricane.
不知是自己，还是身下的土地都在摇晃。耳旁混乱的轰鸣让人感觉如同被抛向了一场飓风。

Marsha: Sonetto, is the Timekeeper alright?
玛尔纱：十四行诗，司辰还好吗？

Sonetto: She's awake!
十四行诗：司辰……她醒过来了！

Vertin: Sonetto, Marsha?
维尔汀：……十四行诗，玛尔纱？

Her mind still lingers in another time while her eyes struggle to grasp where she truly is.
大脑还停留在另一个时空，眼睛已经开始确定身体的位置。

Vertin: What's happening? Is Bereggrad ...
维尔汀：到底发生什么事了？别列格勒……

Sonetto: Krutov launched a surprise attack on Bereggrad an hour ago. Some bandits have broken into the city under the cover of their air force.
十四行诗：别列格勒在一小时前遭到格鲁托夫军队的突然袭击，一些匪军的士兵在空中轰炸的掩护下进入了城市。

Sonetto: The cavalry and the militia have formed a defense. Igor went to contact his people for reinforcements. We need to get you to safety as soon as possible.
十四行诗：骑兵军和民兵正在防守城市，伊戈尔也去联络自己的军队增援了。我们需要尽快转移到安全地带。

Vertin: How long was I down there? 15 minutes, right?
维尔汀：我应该……只在下面待了十五分钟才对。

Sonetto: 15 minutes? Timekeeper, you ... you've been gone for almost 12 hours. I just brought you back above ground.
十四行诗：不，司辰，您已经……在下面消失了十二个小时了。我刚刚才把您从地下带出来。

Vertin: ...
维尔汀：……

Marsha: Timekeeper, sorry for asking so soon, but are you able to move?
玛尔纱：司辰，虽然现在这么问有些勉强，但是——你可以行动了吗？

Vertin: Yes, I'm fine. Just give me one more minute.
维尔汀：我没问题，马上……就能站起来。

Marsha: Alright. Here's the situation: Krutov's vanguard has occupied part of the outskirts.
玛尔纱：好的，眼下的情况是，格鲁托夫攻城的先锋部队占领了部分外围城区。

Marsha: Though this church is currently within the safe zone, it won't be for long. We need to retreat a few streets back.
玛尔纱：这座小修道院还在友军控制区内，但是坚持不了多久了。我们需要撤退到下一个街区。

Marsha: Ten minutes ago, a small force cut off our retreat. I'll attempt to break through a route connecting us to the militia's stronghold.
玛尔纱：有一支小部队在十分钟前切断了我们的退路，稍后我会尝试突围，打开一条连接修道院和民兵据点的路线。

Marsha: Wait for my signal, Timekeeper.
玛尔纱：等待我的信号，司辰！

Marsha: ...!!
玛尔纱：——！

The knight's trench club proves an effective weapon in the cramped alleys of the city. It isn't long before Marsha has cleared a path.
堑壕棒不失为狭小空间中肉搏的利器，骑士很快在有敌军把守的巷道中打开了一条通路。

Marsha: Timekeeper! Sonetto! This way, quick!
玛尔纱：司辰，十四行诗，快到我这儿来！

Sonetto: Timekeeper, please hold onto me.
十四行诗：司辰，请抓紧我。

Vertin: Alright.
维尔汀：嗯……

Even with half her weight on her, Sonetto manages to cross the open danger of the main street with astonishing speed.
一半的重量压在第一助手身上，她仍然以惊人的速度穿越了开阔而危险的主要街道。

Marsha: At the other end of this alley is the area under our control. The Cavalry Corps will escort you out of the city to a safe place.
玛尔纱：巷子的另一头就是骑兵军和别列格勒民兵占领的街区了，他们会带你们出城去安全的地方。

Sonetto: Aren't you leaving with us, Ms. Marsha?
十四行诗：你不和我们一起离开吗，玛尔纱女士？

Marsha: I'll take you to the commander. But after that, I'll stay in Bereggrad until the enemy is repelled.
玛尔纱：我会把你们带到军长身边，但是在敌军从城市里被赶出去之前，我会留在别列格勒，为这里的人出一份力。

The wreckage of a warplane shot down just moments ago by Igor's anti-aircraft guns lies lodged in the dome of a church.
轰炸机的残骸插在修道院的圆顶上，是刚刚被伊戈尔部队的防空高炮击落的。

Before that, several shells had been launched into the militia camp, hospitals, churches, and residences, setting the hard-kept peace ablaze.
在此之前，它和它的伙伴已经将数枚炮弹送到了民兵营地、医院、修道院和民居，声势浩大地打破了堪堪维持住的平静。

Merel: Friendlies! It's friendlies! They're back!
梅蕾尔：自己人，是自己人，她们回来了！

Merel lowers her binoculars and calls for the militia guarding the street fortifications to clear a path, allowing the three young women to fall back into the rear formation.
梅蕾尔放下望远镜，呼唤着让守卫街垒的民兵让出一条路，把一支刚从交火线下来的队伍放进防线后方。

The commander strides forward at the sound of her voice.
留守城内的指挥官闻声而至。

Commander Ivan: Are you alright, Timekeeper? You don't seem well.
军长伊万：你受伤了吗，司辰，看起来你的状态很不好？

Vertin: It's okay. I'm not hurt. Just a bit dizzy.
维尔汀：没关系，我没有受伤，只是有点头晕。

Sonetto: The Timekeeper hasn't eaten anything for over 12 hours. Her blood sugar might be the problem. I'll take her to have some food.
十四行诗：司辰在地下待了十二个小时，没有吃任何东西，她的血糖值可能会因此出问题。我先带她去吃点东西。

Commander Ivan: I see. When you finish, take her to the sich outside of the city.
军长伊万：十四行诗小姐，司辰就交给你照顾了。我希望你能把她带到城外的骑兵军塞契。

Commander Ivan: This fight will not end soon. The city is no place to linger.
军长伊万：别列格勒的巷战还会持续很久，还有随时可能到来的空袭，这里并不安全。

Sonetto: Yes, Commander. I'll take her away as soon as possible.
十四行诗：好的，我很快会带司辰离开。

Commander Ivan: Make it so.
军长伊万：那就这样办。

The commander nods and turns to the knight waiting nearby. Merel is standing at her side, unnoticed until now.
军长点了点头，转向等候在旁的骑士——不知什么时候，被骑兵军救下的落水少女已经站在她身旁。

Commander Ivan: And you. You swore to stay in Bereggrad.
军长伊万：你之前说会留在别列格勒。

Marsha: Yes, sir. I can join the militia rescue team. Many medical staff in the city cannot defend themselves.
玛尔纱：是，我可以加入民兵团的救援队，很多留在城市里的医生没有自保的能力。

Commander Ivan: Good. Medics of the Cavalry Corps and Gryphon Legion will stay as well. You'll aid each other.
军长伊万：很好，骑兵军与狮鹫军的部分军医也会留在城内，希望你们能互相帮助。

Marsha: Of course.
玛尔纱：没问题。

Merel: I-I'm coming with you, Marsha.
梅蕾尔：我、我也跟你一起，玛尔纱。

Marsha: Fine, but do as I say.
玛尔纱：那你就认真听我说的话。

Merel: Okay.
梅蕾尔：好……

The commander looks around for Igor. The two leaders agreed to stay in the city.
此刻，军长已经回头去找同样留在城里的伊戈尔。

Igor is standing nearby, still holding a telescope, his gaze fixed on the city gate.
后者依然举着望远镜，正看向城门口。

He doesn't move for a long time.
将军注视城门的时间很长。

Commander Ivan: What do you see?
军长伊万：你在看什么？

Beyond the gate lies only the vast golden plain.
城门之外只有一大片一大片金色的原野。

Igor: I know Krutov. If this is his declaration of war, he will not stop at bombs and bombers.
伊戈尔：我了解格鲁托夫，他要想宣战，不会只送我们几枚炮弹和一架轰炸机。

Commander Ivan: What? You mean ...
军长伊万：什么意思？你是说……

Igor: It's coming.
伊戈尔：来了。

The grass bows low as black galloping hooves carve a path straight to the city.
金黄的茅草伏低了下去，被疾驰的马蹄踩出的路直直地向城门而来。

A cloth-covered rider bounces limply on the horse's back. He carries a blood-smeared javelin, like a Roman priest riding toward Carthage.
黑马上的骑士蒙着白布，握着一把染血的镖枪，正如光临迦太基城下的罗马祭司。

Igor: A messenger bearing a declaration of war. Hm. A classic move.
伊戈尔：派来宣战的信使，真是古典的桥段。

Commander Ivan: A pity the man doesn't know how to ride.
军长伊万：可惜派来了一个不会骑马的骑兵。

The messenger does not grip the reins. He jostles and sways, like an ominous banner battered too strongly by the wind. But the black horse only gallops faster, pummeling straight for the gate.
信使没有抓住缰绳，在风中摇摇欲坠，如一面不祥的旗帜。黑马兀自向城门奔驰而来。

Marsha: That's ...
玛尔纱：这个人……

By the time the messenger arrives, the cavalry have already formed ranks, guns trained on him.
当信使与他的黑马来到城门口，骑兵们已经一字排开，用枪口迎接他。

But the messenger reads no declaration of war, nor does he throw the bloodstained javelin.
但信使既没来得及朗读宣战书，也没有掷下染血的镖枪。

He only slides from the horse and crashes to the ground, his shattered form laid bare. The horse rears and lets out a mournful whinny.
不多时，信使坠向地面，黑马在一旁徒劳而哀苦地嘶吼着。

Merel: Charon? Andreas?!
梅蕾尔：……卡戎，安德烈森？！

Marsha: I-Is that really—
玛尔纱：真的是——

They finally realize who it is. They rush to the gate, charging through the ring of cavalrymen.
红发骑士与她的同伴终于看清了来人的样子。
她们一起冲向城门口，破开骑兵的包围。

Merel: ...!
梅蕾尔：——！

The instant Merel lays eyes on Charon, she crumples to the ground, her arms wrapped tightly around her head.
看见卡戎的第一眼，梅蕾尔陡然跌坐在地，双手紧紧抱住自己的脑袋。

Merel: No. Don't—!
梅蕾尔：不……不要——！

The cavalry barely manages to hold formation as the warhorses shy back, champing and stamping their feet. They, too, can smell the stench of "death."
骑兵们勉强围成一圈，而战马惊恐地往后退。它们也嗅到了“死亡”的气息。

Marsha: Something isn't right. Don't get close, understood? Don't look at him! Merel, come to me!
玛尔纱：不对——不要靠近，快散开，不要去看他！梅蕾尔，到我这儿来！

She lunges to Merel's side and drags her behind cover, blocking her view of Charon.
她扑向梅蕾尔，将她拖到一处掩体之后，隔绝她与卡戎之间的视线。

The cavalrymen scatter as wave after wave of nauseating howling washes over them.
其他骑兵也在一阵阵令人作呕的哀嚎声中四散溃逃。

The guards at the city gate have no choice but to fall back. Entry to Bereggrad lies wide open, filled with nothing but the stench of "death."
此时此刻，城门边的守军不得不撤离，别列格勒门户洞开，唯有“死亡”的气息充实其间。

Marsha: *deep breath* Someone needs to take Charon to an enclosed space.
玛尔纱：……必须要有人把卡戎带到封闭的场所。

Marsha: I suppose I'll have to give it a try.
玛尔纱：好吧，也许我可以试试。

Just as she has done countless times before, Marsha draws a deep breath and stills her emotions, paying no heed to what may happen when she finally releases whatever fear she is soon to face.
骑士深吸一口气，像过去千百次那样将情绪冻结。她尽量不去想，今天积累的恐惧要在什么时候释放出来。

She gives Merel a gentle pat on the shoulder and steps out from behind the shelter. She walks toward the abyss of "death," looking directly at Charon's fallen body.
她安抚地拍了拍身旁的梅蕾尔，随后走出掩体，一步步迈向“死亡”笼罩的深渊，直视着卡戎倒地不起的身躯。

The fear in her amygdala is severed, just as promised.
杏仁核中的恐惧感被如期阻断。

Marsha: It's working.
玛尔纱：奏效了。

Marsha: Charon, oh my, what have they done to you?
玛尔纱：卡戎……天呐，他们对你做了什么？

What lies before her is barely recognizable. It isn't a body. It's a shattered, indescribable shell.
在她眼前的是一副无法描摹的碎裂躯壳，好像再也经不起任何更进一步的触碰。

It is only by virtue of the blood-soaked ropes that he has managed to maintain any semblance of a human form.
卡戎显然是被绑在马上送到别列格勒来的——感谢这些染血的绳索，他才堪堪维持住了类似于人的形状。

With his coverings gone, and freed from the constraints of a complete form, "death" seeps out from beneath Charon's clothes.
失去了遮掩物，又脱离了完整躯体的压制，“死亡”正丝丝缕缕从收尸人的衣袍下溢出。

The soil beneath him visibly darkens, the air around it swallowed in a heavy fog. Krutov has brought a declaration of death to Bereggrad.
亡者身下的土地正肉眼可见地被浓雾浸染，黑色的军阀将“死亡”的宣告送至别列格勒。

Charon: ...
卡戎：……

Marsha: Yes, Charon? What is it?
玛尔纱：你刚刚是在说话吗，卡戎？

Marsha leans in close in an attempt to catch whatever sound his ruined body is still able to give voice to.
玛尔纱俯下身，离卡戎更近了一点，以便听到他朽坏的身躯尚且能发出的声音。

Charon: A message from Krutov.
卡戎：……一个不幸的消息，我不得不……带给城里的人们……

Charon: He swears to destroy Bereggrad, unless ...
卡戎：格鲁托夫的军队马上就要攻破别列格勒，他……会将城市夷为平地……除非……

Charon: You hand over Igor.
卡戎：……交出伊戈尔。

22  安眠 / Soothing Rest

Seems you've got some iron beneath that calm surface.
？？？：你一定是个有强大心灵的人。

Not everyone would sleep so soundly in a stranger's house.
？？？：一般人无法像你这样在外人的家里睡上一整天。

Honestly, you could've just made yourself at home and stayed the night. When I take out the trash later, I'll be sure to toss you out along with it.
？？？：稍后我会把你和饭后垃圾一起扔出去，为什么不再睡一会儿呢？

选项 / Choices

1. I had a dream.
1. 我做了个梦

2. Did I miss dinner?
2. 我错过了晚餐？

分支 1 / Branch 1

Oh, spare me the details!
？？？：我请求你，不要讲述你的美梦。

I'm sure it was full of enviable warmth and radiance—just enough to scorch my miserable, frozen heart.
？？？：那太过耀眼和温暖，可能会灼伤我冰冷的心。

Whatever you saw or did in that dream, do yourself a favor and let it go.
？？？：不管你在梦里经历了什么做了什么，都请让它过去吧。

分支 2 / Branch 2

You did—and I owe you my thanks.
？？？：是的，感谢你的恩典。

Your sleep talking added a bit of flavor to my otherwise bland meal.
？？？：今天寡淡的晚餐全靠你梦中的呢喃来增添风味。

I haven't had such an appealing appetizer in a long time.
？？？：我很久没喝过那么有滋味的汤了。

选项 / Choices

1. What about my meal?
1. 我的那份呢？

The soup's gone cold. That ship's sailed.
？？？：过去的晚餐已经过去了，你没法再让它回来。

You may not like the reality served on your silver platter—no one does—but you'll have to swallow it.
？？？：过去的事只能留在回忆里，眼下乱套的样子恐怕谁都不想要。

Blame this mess on the black robes if you want; those cowards keep trying to drag the world back to their "good old days."
？？？：而这都拜那些放不下过去的黑袍子所赐。当下的生活不如意就让懦夫们陷入了无止境的怀旧当中。

And the white robes preaching the future? No less clowns for their taste in uniforms—falling off their high horses again and again, brought down by their own arrogance.
？？？：眼里只有未来的白袍子们也都是废物，恐怕是头抬得太高了，才一个劲儿地摔跟头。

Who would have thought? The whole universe turned into one circus starring two troupes of fools.
？？？：这样的两伙人居然能扭打在一起，在世纪末把全宇宙搅得天翻地覆。

Why didn't that blind woman just let the world burn back then? It wouldn't have been worse than these chaotic times. And who asked her to play the martyr, anyway?
？？？：那个盲眼女人当时真不如放着世界毁灭，起码不会比这乱成一团的时间更糟，谁求她自我牺牲了吗？

If you're lucky enough to find her, take her home—and make sure she stays there, quietly, for everyone's sake.
？？？：如果你真的有机会抓住她，请记得带她回家，让她安度晚年吧，这样对大家都好。

共同接续 / Shared continuation

Soothing Rest
安眠

23  沉眠于骨音 / Sleep in the Sound of Bones

The Lost Diary of a Captive
被遗落的俘虏日记

[An old diary with black, viscous stains smeared across its cover. The corners are torn and dog-eared. It seems the owner abandoned it recently.]
November 11
    Every night since my capture, I've heard terrifying, fragmented whispers coming from outside ... and screams—not just one or two people, but many, shrieking and wailing in pain. I can also smell something burning and rotting. Damn it, are those Manus dogs burning corpses? Or performing some twisted ritual?
...
November XX
    I've lost track of the date. Time and life hold no meaning in this godforsaken place. I once saved lives as a doctor ... now, they've used my medical instruments to brutally murder Boyaryshkin, Artyom, and Malyekhov right in front of me. I don't care anymore. If they're going to come for me eventually, they might as well hurry and end it now.
...
November XX
    I don't know how to write this. I actually survived that nightmare—which feels like a curse in itself. We were herded onto an empty clearing where a koldunya in black robes was chanting a song. I could barely make out the words: "... Inculcator of Arcanum, let me manifest decay anew." She pulled away the shroud covering the corpse. As the black mist dissipated, a broken body lay before us.
    She handed me my old scalpel: "Dissect it." I looked down at the remains exposed to the air. Male, early twenties. Mottled, wrinkled skin. Ribs collapsed and fractured. I didn't know what I was doing—fear made me forget everything I once believed. Standing beside her, I raised the blade and started cutting. The scraping of bone was monotonous, but I freed that soul from its mortal prison.
    When I lowered the knife, I realized we were surrounded by a black fog reeking of death. All the other captives had died, their faces twisted in agony. The koldunya seized my filthy hand and laughed: "Thank you, Charon! You helped me unravel death and command it, master Death, and aid its power!"
...
November XX
    Boyaryshkin, Artyom, Malyekhov—they came for me! I didn't mean to! I'm sorry, I'm sorry, I'm sorry ...
[The remaining text is obscured by black, viscous stains.]
[一本被黑色粘稠污渍糊住了封皮的破旧日记本，日记本的角破损、卷折，它似乎离开主人不久。]
十一月十一日
    被俘以后的每个晚上，我都会听到外面传来一些恐怖的细碎低喃声……还有惨叫声——不止一个人、两个人，是很多人在凄厉嘶吼、哀嚎。我还能闻到东西烧焦以及腐坏的味道。该死的，难道这帮重塑之手的走狗们放火烧了尸体？还是在做什么诡异的仪式？
    ……
十一月某日
    我已经不清楚日期了，在这个破败的地方，时间、生命都不再重要。我曾经是救死扶伤的医生，然而他们就在我的面前，用我的医疗器械残暴地夺走了包亚雷什金、阿尔乔姆和麦列霍夫的生命。我已经无所谓了，如果他们终于来带走我，那就赶快来吧。
    ……
十一月某日
    我不知该如何下笔。我竟从那样一场噩梦中幸存——这本身就像一种诅咒。我们被驱赶到一片空地上，墨袍女巫吟唱着歌谣，我依稀能听到：“……秘之教诲者，令我复现腐败。”而当她揭开那具尸体上的覆布时，随着黑雾的消逝，呈现在我们面前的是一副残躯。
    她竟将我旧日的手术刀递还给我：“解剖它。”我低下头看着那具暴露在空气中的遗骸，男性，二十岁上下，皮肤斑驳起皱，肋骨塌陷骨折。我不知道自己在做什么，恐惧使我忘记了之前的信念。在她身旁，我举起刀一点点划落。骨擦音很单调，但我为这副魂魄脱去了禁锢。
    当我放下刀，才发现四周弥漫着死亡的黑雾。所有俘虏都已气绝，面目扭曲。那女巫却举起我沾满污秽的手，放声大笑：“多谢，卡戎！你令我解开死亡，驭使死亡！助益祂的大能！”
    ……
十一月某日
    包亚雷什金，阿尔乔姆，麦列霍夫，他们来找我了！我不是故意的！对不起，对不起，对不起……
[后面的字迹被掩盖在黑色黏稠污渍下。]

24  生活与命运 / Life and Fate

Life and Fate
生活与命运

Militia Base, Bereggrad
别列格勒·民兵营

He does not feel pain as the living do, but it has left its mark all the same. He must have endured unimaginable torment.
他不像生者那般会感受到痛苦，但痛苦很明显在他身上留下了烙印。他一定经历过难以想象的折磨。

Marsha: His bodily structure is too fractured to store his arcane energy. The leakage is worsening his condition.
玛尔纱：整个身体的结构都被破坏了，神秘术能量严重外泄，这进一步导致了虚弱……

Charon's body is held together in an unnatural way. Like a man crushed a thousand times, his fragments no longer fit together as they should.
卡戎身躯的组合方式十分别扭，像一个人被粉碎了一千次，各个零件无法如常拼合到一起。

Marsha: How could the Manus do such a thing?
玛尔纱：重塑之手怎么能做出这种事。

Merel: Why? He's got nothing to do with any of this.
梅蕾尔：为什么……他明明和这个地方毫无关系，为什么连卡戎也被……

Merel stands with her back to Charon, her shoulders hunched and trembling.
梅蕾尔背向着卡戎站立，她缩起的肩膀正微微发颤。

Merel: I should've gone with you, Charon.
梅蕾尔：我应该和你一起走的，卡戎。

Charon now lies on a stretcher. Silent and contained, like a black wall behind them.
和此地毫无瓜葛的朋友正躺在担架上。他总是沉默、自持，像一堵墙，静静伫立在所有人身后。

Merel finally saw Charon's arcane skill for the first time. Vast and hollow, like death itself.
经历漫长旅途之后，梅蕾尔今天第一次见到了卡戎的神秘术——庞大，空洞，如同死亡本身。

Merel: No ...
梅蕾尔：不……

Marsha: Merel. Merel?
玛尔纱：梅蕾尔……梅蕾尔？

Merel: Marsha, *sniffle* I ...
梅蕾尔：玛尔纱……我——

She instinctively turns to her, but Marsha grips her shoulders firmly, holding her back in place.
她本能地想回头，红发骑士的双手立刻扣在她的肩上，把她拧了回去。

Marsha: Don't turn around. I think Charon wants to tell you something.
玛尔纱：别转身，我只是想提醒你打起精神，卡戎似乎想说些什么。

Merel: What? What is it, Charon?
梅蕾尔：你……你想说什么，卡戎？

Charon: Merel ...
卡戎：……梅蕾尔……

Merel: *sob* Charon, I'm sorry! It's all my fault!
梅蕾尔：别、别说了，是我不好！

With a single word, Merel's words come rushing like a river breaking its banks.
仅仅一个词语，就让她的话如决堤河水，汹涌而出。

Merel: You protected me, walked with me all this way, hid me, kept me safe, and ... and after all that, after everything you've done for me, I left you there all alone!
梅蕾尔：我们一起走了这么久，这么远，我害你和我一起东躲西藏，最后……最后遇到危险我就丢下你不管了。

Merel: If only I weren't such a weakling, if only I were brave like the others, I could've taken you with me, saved you for once.
梅蕾尔：我要是再厉害一点，就能把你一起带走，或者从别人手上把你救回来，可是……

Merel: But ... but ...
梅蕾尔：可是……可是……

Merel: It's all my fault. I'm useless. I should never have left you behind. If it weren't for me, you could've ... you could've ...
梅蕾尔：是我太没有用了……对不起，是我害了你和安德烈森……对不起，我……你可能不会变成这样的——

Merel: *whimper*
梅蕾尔：呜……

The words choke in her throat before they can be spoken.
更多的话还没有说出口，它们全都一股脑地哽在她的喉咙里。

Charon: No, Merel ...
卡戎：……不，梅蕾尔……

Charon finds a chance to piece together his broken speech.
卡戎这才有机会重拾破碎的言语。

Charon: You promised to run away when danger came.
卡戎：你答应过我，遇见危险就会逃走……

Charon: Why do you remain here? Go. Find a safe place.
卡戎：为什么你现在还……留在这里？为什么不去更远的……更安全的地方……？

Merel: No. I can't. I can't run away anymore. I shouldn't.
梅蕾尔：但我……已经没办法逃跑了。不，我不应该逃走的。

Merel: I should never have run in the first place.
梅蕾尔：从、从最开始，我就不应该逃走的。

Merel: If I'd just gone back to the squad, if I hadn't asked to go with you, if ...
梅蕾尔：如果我老老实实地归队，如果我没说要和你一起走……如果……

Merel: *whimper*
梅蕾尔：呜……呜……

Merel is too overwhelmed to speak.
梅蕾尔又伤心得说不出话了。

A pair of hands gently wipes the tears from her face.
有一双手拭去了她满面的泪水。

Marsha: Hush now. How are you supposed to help me fix Charon up if you keep crying like this?
玛尔纱：你哭成这样，我还怎么指望你和我一起把卡戎治好？

Merel: ...
梅蕾尔：……

Marsha: Don't forget what we need to do.
玛尔纱：别忘了我们要做的事。

Marsha: Charon will be fine. As long as we can restore the vessel of his consciousness, he'll recover.
玛尔纱：卡戎不会有事的，只要意识的载体能够复原，他应该就能慢慢恢复过来。

Marsha: I'm the only one who can look at him right now, and I need a reliable assistant. Only the two of us can help him. Do you understand?
玛尔纱：现在只有我能面对卡戎，而我需要一个信得过的助手，也就是说，这里能帮他的只有我们两个了，明白了吗？

Merel: Yes. *sniff* Right. I understand.
梅蕾尔：对……没错，我明白。

Marsha: Then take a moment and wipe your tears.
玛尔纱：那么，休息一下，擦擦眼泪。

Marsha: It won't be an easy task, but it'll soon be over.
玛尔纱：整个过程不会很轻松，但很快就会结束。

Marsha: Just follow my instructions and give me what I need when I ask for it. Can you do that, Merel?
玛尔纱：听我的指令，我要什么东西就立刻递给我，能做到吗，梅蕾尔？

A boy steps onto a fallen stone pillar, the wreckage of the warplane in the church behind him.
少年踩在倒塌的石柱上，身后是插在修道院房顶的轰炸机。

It's been shot down, yet it still seems to posture in defiance—a grim reminder of how fragile this city is.
飞机被击落了，但它的身子依然耀武扬威，时刻提醒着这座城市有多么脆弱。

Citizen Representative: We will not be victims!
市民代表：别列格勒不能成为牺牲品！

Citizens: We will not be victims!
市民们：别列格勒不能成为牺牲品！

One by one, people gather around Fyodor Petrovich, quickly forming a tight circle.
许多人围着费奥多尔·彼得罗维奇，彼此紧挨着，成了一个紧密的圈。

Citizen Representative: Save Bereggrad! Hand over Igor!
市民代表：保护别列格勒！交出伊戈尔！

Citizens: Hand over Igor!
市民们：交出伊戈尔！

More and more voices join the chanting.
不断有零星的人靠近他们，成为圈的一部分。

The rest of the residents linger at a distance. Some whisper behind covered mouths. Others lift anxious eyes to the sky, searching for bombs that may yet fall.
剩下的居民在不远的地方看着，没有加入示威的圆环。有人掩面私语，有人抬起头，忧心忡忡地看着即将落下炸弹的天空。

The chanting crowd clambers over the street barricades, only to be halted by gun barrels before the fortifications of the city defense headquarters.
示威的市民翻越街巷上堆砌的障碍物，终于在城防指挥部前的街垒前被黑洞洞的枪口拦下。

Sergey: Stop! Don't go any further!
谢尔盖：别往前走！

Citizen Representative: Stand aside, kid. I need to talk to your commander.
市民代表：滚开，你这胡子还没长齐的小毛孩。我要和你们的军长说话。

Sergey: You can tell me. I'll pass it on.
谢尔盖：你要说就在这儿说，我给你传话。

Sergey feels a shove from the side.
谢尔盖察觉到身侧有人推了他一把。

Militiaman: Hey, I know this man. Let me talk to him.
民兵：我认得这人，小子，我来和他说。

The Bereggrad militiaman climbs onto the fortifications, standing taller.
别列格勒民兵攀上街垒，好站得更高些。

Militiaman: Fyodor, take your people and turn back! Or are you here just to cause trouble?
民兵：费奥多尔，带着你的人滚回去吧！还是说，你就是要来给我们添乱的？

Citizen Representative: Still standing strong with the cavalry, are we, Dima? Don't you know how many have sided with us?
市民代表：迪马，你怎么还和骑兵军站在一起？我告诉你吧，民兵团有好多人都站在我们这边了。

Militiaman: Them? Pah, just little sheep following any dog that barks at them!
民兵：他们就是一些没断奶的羊，被人一声吼就吓得四脚朝天了。

Citizen Representative: You idiots! All we have to do is hand over that Igor, and Krutov will leave us be! The choice is obvious!
市民代表：真是说不通道理——只要把那个叫伊戈尔的人交出去，格鲁托夫就不烧咱们的城。为什么不做这个买卖呢？

Sergey: You're the idiot! They're bandits! You can't trust a word they say!
谢尔盖：你这城里人，白痴，土匪的话也听！

Citizen Representative: And this Igor? Who is he?
市民代表：那你又知道这伊戈尔是什么玩意？

Citizen Representative: No one here has ever even heard of him. It's no skin off our back to hand him over. You cavalry said you wanted to protect the city. Now is your chance!
市民代表：顿河上下有谁认识伊戈尔？把他送出去又有什么坏处？骑兵军说要守城，那把伊戈尔交出去，这事不就完了？

Citizen Representative: If Krutov has some personal issue with Igor, then I say they solve it themselves on the steppe!
市民代表：伊戈尔和格鲁托夫有恩怨，就让他们自己到草原上解决。

Sergey: You don't know what you're talking about. The drink's pickled your brain. Just look at your nose.
谢尔盖：你什么都搞不明白，大叔，我一看你这鼻子，就知道你早就把自己的脑子喝坏了。

Sergey: You've got no say here anyway. So shut it and go home.
谢尔盖：别在这里废话了，我听着都头疼。这事要怎么做，难道还是你说了算呀？

A cacophony of boos erupts from the crowd.
人群爆发出一阵嘈杂的、无法辨认内容的嘘声。

Militiaman: Or you can use your enthusiasm to fight off the bandits and get our market back.
民兵：有这股热情劲儿，倒是带你们的人去把土匪占着的鱼市场夺回来啊！

The commander turns from the window.
军长收回了投向窗外的目光。

Commander Ivan: Tell the first battalion's commander to evacuate the citizens trapped outside. Just a few sharp soldiers. Don't worsen the situation.
军长伊万：告诉一营营长，让他带上几个机灵点儿的兵去疏散堵在外面的市民。

Commander Ivan: Be careful. No misfires. Fyodor won't dare start a real fight. He'd sooner roll in his wealth than die.
军长伊万：记住，别擦枪走火了。费奥多尔不敢真的动手，他还要留着命享受家里数不完的钱。

Sternova: Yes, Commander.
斯捷尔诺娃：是。

Sternova leaves at the order.
斯捷尔诺娃带着命令离去。

Commander Ivan: You taught Krutov well, Igor.
军长伊万：格鲁托夫不愧是你的人，伊戈尔。

Commander Ivan: He has driven a wedge between us. Old-fashioned, but effective. He knows we need both the militia and the citizens to hold Bereggrad.
军长伊万：这离间的计策老套但有效，我们的士兵不擅长巷战，保卫别列格勒需要依靠民兵团和市民的配合。

Commander Ivan: One whispered deal, and half turn against us.
军长伊万：格鲁托夫把你的名字抛出来后，至少有一半人失去了对我们的信任。

Igor: He will do anything to bring me a humiliating death.
伊戈尔：他会用一切方法让我死得更狼狈一点。

Commander Ivan: Why? I never warmed to him, true, but I had no inkling his hatred ran so deep.
军长伊万：为什么格鲁托夫这么执着地要你的命？我以前确实和他处不来，看不出他这么恨你。

Igor falls silent, as if fishing his thoughts from the depths of a dark pond.
伊戈尔沉默半晌，像是要在一处黑暗的深潭中捞取思绪。

Igor: Like me, there was no turning back for him after São Paulo. He can only continue down the path he has laid.
伊戈尔：他和我一样，在圣保罗之后都无法再回头，只能在认定的路上走下去。

Igor: Since the deal with Manus Vindictae, he has resented me. He claims I shorted him for his sacrifice.
伊戈尔：与重塑之手达成一次性的交易之后，他怨恨我给的不够多，不值得他与我一同做出那些所谓“正确的牺牲”。

Igor: He also believes our break from the Manus was a grave mistake. He wishes to wrest more power from them.
伊戈尔：格鲁托夫认为脱离重塑之手是一个巨大的错误，他希望从他们身上攫取更多力量。

Igor: But I never promised him power; I promised him a chance at survival. Still, he calls the "boon" a sacred thing that will grant him the power of a god.
伊戈尔：不过，我承诺给他的从来都不是力量，而是生存。但他仍然不明白，以为“奇迹”是能让他接近神明的圣物。

Igor: Krutov took my choice as weakness. Now, he is obsessed with proving that his path is the right one.
伊戈尔：格鲁托夫把这种观念不合当成是我的软弱，执着于向我证明他的道路才称得上正确。

Igor: He wants to kill me, to take the "boon," to prove his worth to the Manus.
伊戈尔：……他一直都想杀死我，夺走我向重塑之手换来的“奇迹”，或者是向重塑之手证明自己的价值。

Igor: We can take advantage of this.
伊戈尔：所以，我们可以利用这种“执着”。

Outside, arguments between the cavalrymen and citizens rise and fall along the street.
窗外的街道上，已经传来骑兵军与市民发生争执的声音。

Igor: One thing is certain: this decisive battle will not be inside the city. We cannot stay here long.
伊戈尔：有一点是没错的，决定性的战斗会在郊外发生。我们不能在城内久留。

Igor: Let us leave together, Ivan. We will discuss our strategy on the way to camp.
伊戈尔：一起出城吧，伊万。我们最好在回营地的路上就找到击败格鲁托夫的办法。

25  诀别书 / Farewell Letter

Farewell Letter
诀别书

Sternova: So, you're saying you devised this plan with Igor?
斯捷尔诺娃：……那么，这就是你和伊戈尔商议的作战计划？

Sternova stands opposite the commander, the map table between them. Agitated, she shifts the markers representing the military forces back and forth.
斯捷尔诺娃与军长隔着地图桌相对而立，她焦躁地将用来代表军事力量的指示物挪来挪去。

The commander glances up at her. Her words are minimal, but her stance is made clear.
军长抬头看了她一眼，尽管后者并未言明，但她的态度不言而喻。

Commander Ivan: Put the gryphon down, Sabina. We're all in the same boat now.
军长伊万：把那个狮鹫放下吧，斯捷尔诺娃，现在我们和伊戈尔站在同一条船上。

Sternova raises a hand and sweeps the wooden gryphon to the edge of the table.
斯捷尔诺娃挥了挥手，把狮鹫木雕甩到了地图的边缘。

Sternova: I'm not questioning your orders, but I will state for the record that leaving our back wide open to Igor is too risky.
斯捷尔诺娃：我非常认同你提出的作战计划，军长，但我仍然不认为可以放心地把后背交给伊戈尔。

Sternova: After dark, the cavalry will advance upstream with minimal gear to launch a surprise attack, cutting off Krutov's supply lines.
斯捷尔诺娃：天黑后，让骑兵军放弃辎重，沿左岸向上游发动奇袭，清扫所有遭遇的敌方部队并切断格鲁托夫的补给路线。

Sternova: It makes sense. Our riders know the terrain like the back of their hand; this will allow us to capitalize on our superior mobility.
斯捷尔诺娃：——我不怀疑计划的可行性，骑兵们熟悉河岸的田地与山谷，我军的机动性能得到最大程度的发挥。

Sternova: But we would be leaving the right bank, the forest, the sich, and even Bereggrad exposed.
斯捷尔诺娃：但把右岸林地、塞契与别列格勒的城防交给伊戈尔，让他来保护我们的后方——

Sternova: We're throwing ourselves into the old lion's mouth and praying he won't bite!
斯捷尔诺娃：这和把羊肉送到狮子嘴里有什么区别？只要伊戈尔愿意，骑兵军随时会身处腹背受敌的境况。

Commander Ivan: You're right. We'd be exposed. But a coalition without trust wins nothing.
军长伊万：如果同盟的两军不能互相信任，那只会被逐一击破，任何作战计划都无从谈起。

Commander Ivan: You've seen Krutov and his army. Neither the Cavalry Corps nor the militia can meet his air power or artillery.
军长伊万：你已经见识过格鲁托夫的军队了，骑兵军和民兵团都没有能力应付敌人的空中力量和地面的重武器。

Commander Ivan: Bereggrad would be rubble if not for Igor's anti-air guns.
军长伊万：如果没有伊戈尔带来的几台高射炮，别列格勒在第一轮空袭中就能被炸成废墟。

Sternova: ...
斯捷尔诺娃：……

Her thoughts drift to the poorhouse, its dome half-destroyed in a bomb dropped during their withdrawal from Bereggrad.
她想起撤离别列格勒时，印在视野中的被航空炸弹削去半边拱顶的救济院。

Commander Ivan: The Gryphon Legion is well-equipped, but they're thin on numbers and supplies. They will hold cities and forests best.
军长伊万：狮鹫军装备精良，但人数不多，而且没有建立完备的补给线。他们更适合依托城市和林地构筑防线。

Commander Ivan: Let them guard the city. Our riders will flank the enemy by night. Both Igor and I agree on this.
军长伊万：所以，把城市和林地交给伊戈尔，骑兵军从侧翼找机会袭击敌军，我和伊戈尔都认为这是一个合理的选择。

Sternova: But ...
斯捷尔诺娃：但……

She knows the commander has reasons, yet the smoke of South America still hangs heavy in her memory, casting an inescapable shadow over this coalition.
她变得有些迟疑。她知道长官是对的，可南美洲的硝烟仍是笼罩在这场合作之上的阴霾。

Commander Ivan: I hear your concerns, Sabina.
军长伊万：我理解你的疑虑。

He picks up the wooden gryphon and sets it down on the sich.
军长拿起狮鹫木雕，让它降落在塞契。

Commander Ivan: When the operation begins, Igor will come to our sich. All his communication will be monitored.
军长伊万：伊戈尔本人会留在骑兵军塞契，不会前往前线。而他与麾下部队的每一次通话都会处于我们的监控之下。

Sternova: How? I doubt our operators can tap his lines so effectively.
斯捷尔诺娃：我们的联络电台没有侵入伊戈尔方面线路的能力，要怎么窃听他？

Commander Ivan: There will be no such need. On our return from the city, I asked Igor to use only our lines for the operation.
军长伊万：不，我们不需要窃听他。在回营地的路上，我已经向伊戈尔提过公开通讯的要求。

Commander Ivan: He agreed.
军长伊万：他同意了。

Sternova: ...
斯捷尔诺娃：……

Sternova nods hesitantly.
斯捷尔诺娃迟疑地点了点头。

Sternova: I see. Still, I'll be keeping an eye on him.
斯捷尔诺娃：我还是会盯着他的。

Igor: Your scrutiny is welcome, Colonel Sternova.
伊戈尔：——我非常欢迎你的监督，上校。

The rebel leader enters the tent, his presence tightening the already frayed nerves of the chief of staff.
不请自来的叛军将领走入营帐，踩在了参谋长本就紧绷的神经上。

Under Sternova's piercing gaze, he places a document on the table.
伊戈尔顶着斯捷尔诺娃审视的目光，将一份文件放在了沙盘上。

Sternova: What's this?
斯捷尔诺娃：这是什么？
